@@ -63,160 +63,175 @@ function typecheckProbes(project: string): ProbeRun {
   // controls doing their job, on the wrong question. `isAbsolute` is win32's on Windows and posix's
   // on Linux, which is the same platform whose compiler printed the line.
   const isListedFile = (line: string) => isAbsolute(line);
   return {
     status: result.status,
     output: lines.filter((line) => !isListedFile(line)).join('\n'),
     files: lines.filter(isListedFile).map((line) => line.trim()),
   };
 }
 
 /** Did tsc really read this probe? Guards every assertion below against a program matching nothing. */
 function compiled(run: ProbeRun, probeFile: string): boolean {
   return run.files.some((file) => file.endsWith(probeFile));
 }
 
 /**
  * TypeScript reports a blocked merge two different ways: `TS2300 Duplicate identifier 'X'` for a
  * single clash, and one `TS6200` listing every identifier when a file has several at once. Both are
  * the rejection these tests assert, so either is accepted — matching only the first form would make
  * a test pass or fail on how many attacks happen to share a file.
  */
 function rejects(output: string, name: string): boolean {
   if (output.includes(`Duplicate identifier '${name}'`)) return true;
   return output
     .split('\n')
     .filter((line) => line.includes('conflict with those in another file:'))
     .some((line) => line.split(':').pop()!.split(',').some((entry) => entry.trim() === name));
 }
 
 describe('harness invention is rejected by the compiler', () => {
   const probe = typecheckProbes(PROBE_PROJECT);
 
   it('compiles the probes it claims to check', () => {
     // The empty-program control. A config that matches nothing exits 0 and says nothing, so without
     // this every assertion below could be passing because the attack was never read.
     expect(compiled(probe, 'harness-invention.probe.ts'), `${PROBE_PROJECT} did not compile the probe file`).toBe(true);
   });
 
   it('the probe program does not compile — if it ever does, an attack has reopened', () => {
     expect(
       probe.status,
       `${PROBE_PROJECT} compiled clean. Every construct in it is a way for a suite to declare harness ` +
         `members the factory never produces; a green run means one of them is legal again and the ` +
         `type-check is once more able to promise a runtime shape nothing establishes.\n${probe.output}`,
     ).not.toBe(0);
   });
 
   it('rejects a harness type invented through an intersection', () => {
     // `AtHarness & { auditLog }` is a SUBTYPE of AtHarness, so it satisfies any `extends` constraint.
     // The defence is that the harness is not a type parameter at all — hence an arity error.
     expect(probe.output, 'the intersection attack was not rejected by arity').toContain('TS2558');
   });
 
   /**
    * EVERY type that must stay a type alias, and why the list is exhaustive rather than a sample.
    *
    * The rule is uniform — nothing reachable from the harness object, or from the wrapper handed to a
    * test body, may be an interface — so the guard is uniform too. An earlier version of this test
    * asserted three of these and claimed to assert all of them; both Gate 2 reviewers caught that the
    * guard was narrower than its own comment, which is exactly the reversion such a gap invites.
    *
    * THIS ARRAY IS THE SPECIFICATION. The probe file has to carry an augmentation for every name in
    * it, because a name listed here but not attacked there produces no diagnostic and fails its test.
    * So the two files cannot drift apart silently.
    */
   const ALIAS_PROTECTED = [
     // the harness contract itself
     'AtHarness',
     // the capability contracts it references (contracts.ts)
     'WorldSeam',
     'Fixtures',
     'Clock',
     'Sentinel',
     'Sentinels',
     'FaultHandle',
     'Faults',
     'StaticScan',
     'ProviderAttempt',
     'EmailProviderSim',
     'Vendors',
+    // H4's semantic-oracle contracts (contracts.ts) — every shape `judge()` takes or returns, not
+    // only the seam itself, because a rubric or a verdict is handed to a test body just as `h` is
+    'SemanticCriterion',
+    'ExtractionSpec',
+    'NumericToleranceComparator',
+    'CountAtLeastComparator',
+    'CriterionComparator',
+    'ExtractionCriterion',
+    'RubricCriterion',
+    'Rubric',
+    'VoteTally',
+    'CriterionVerdict',
+    'VerdictProvenance',
+    'SemanticVerdict',
+    'SemanticOracle',
     // the config seam (config.ts)
     'ConfigRegistry',
     // the wrapper types every test body holds directly (registry.ts) — including the world itself,
     // because `open()` hands `w` to the body exactly as it hands `h`
     'OpenWorld',
     'AtContext',
     'WorldLike',
   ];
 
   for (const contract of ALIAS_PROTECTED) {
     it(`rejects a member merged into ${contract}`, () => {
       expect(
         rejects(probe.output, contract),
         `${contract} accepted a merged-in member, so it is an interface again. A suite can now declare ` +
           `a member on it and read that member green against a value that never supplies it.\n${probe.output}`,
       ).toBe(true);
     });
   }
 });

 136: export type Vendors<Channel extends string = string> = {
 137:   email: EmailProviderSim<Channel>;
 138: };
 139: 
 140: /* -------------------------------------------------------------- H4 semantic oracles */
 141: 
 142: /**
 143:  * Judging the MEANING of generated text, where string-matching cannot decide.
 144:  *
 145:  * Three ratified acceptance criteria need this: AT-009.07 (rejection copy instructs and never
 146:  * accuses), AT-004.10 (Discovery output satisfies a fixture-specific semantic oracle), AT-033.07
 147:  * (the assistant's four framed answers, each with a pinned oracle). NONE of their suites exists
 148:  * yet, so what the ratified text pins today is not any finished rubric â€” it is the set of
 149:  * criterion KINDS the contract has to be able to express: semantic-absence, semantic-containment,
 150:  * count-minimum, numeric-tolerance, no-fabrication. That set is what the shapes below are derived
 151:  * from, and `harness/rubrics/` says plainly which of its examples are near-final and which are
 152:  * disposable skeletons.
 153:  *
 154:  * THE CRITERION IS A DISCRIMINATED UNION, and that is the load-bearing decision (Gate 1 finding
 155:  * F4). A rubric that only carried prose statements would push every threshold into the judge:
 156:  * "is the runway within 10% of $4,200" is a question about arithmetic, and asking a language model
 157:  * arithmetic questions makes the answer as unstable as the language. So the union splits the work.
 158:  * A `semantic` criterion is a binary question only a reader can answer. An `extraction` criterion
 159:  * asks the judge for a NUMBER and nothing else, and the comparison against the expected value
 160:  * happens in `harness/oracles.ts`, in code, deterministically. The judge is never told the
 161:  * expected value or the tolerance â€” it cannot answer the question it is not asked.
 162:  *
 163:  * Expected values, tolerances and minimums for the real acceptance criteria are supplied by the
 164:  * suites that own those ids, when they are translated. What ships here is the SHAPE, with the
 165:  * boundary cases (a difference exactly equal to the tolerance, a count exactly one below the
 166:  * minimum) pinned in `oracles.selftest.ts`.
 167:  */
 168: 
 169: /** A binary question about the material that only a reader can settle. */
 170: export type SemanticCriterion = {
 171:   kind: 'semantic';
 172:   id: string;
 173:   /** phrased so that TRUE is the compliant answer â€” the judge answers whether it holds */
 174:   statement: string;
 175:   /** a failing REQUIRED criterion fails the whole verdict; an optional one is reported and no more */
 176:   required: boolean;
 177: };
 178: 
 179: /**
 180:  * What the judge is asked to pull out of the material. `unit` and `normalization` exist because
 181:  * "the runway" and "the runway in months, rounded down" are different questions, and the second
 182:  * one is the answerable one.
 183:  */
 184: export type ExtractionSpec = {
 185:   what: string;
 186:   unit?: string;
 187:   normalization?: string;
 188: };
 189: 
 190: /** |extracted âˆ’ expected| â‰¤ tolerance. Inclusive at the boundary, and conformance pins that. */
 191: export type NumericToleranceComparator = {
 192:   op: 'numeric_within_tolerance';
 193:   expected: number;
 194:   tolerance: number;
 195: };
 196: 
 197: /** extracted â‰¥ minimum, over a count â€” so a fractional extraction is a typed error, not a round. */
 198: export type CountAtLeastComparator = {
 199:   op: 'count_at_least';
 200:   minimum: number;
 201: };
 202: 
 203: export type CriterionComparator = NumericToleranceComparator | CountAtLeastComparator;
 204: 
 205: /** The judge extracts; `oracles.ts` compares. The comparator never reaches the judge. */
 206: export type ExtractionCriterion = {
 207:   kind: 'extraction';
 208:   id: string;
 209:   statement: string;
 210:   required: boolean;
 211:   extract: ExtractionSpec;
 212:   compare: CriterionComparator;
 213: };
 214: 
 215: export type RubricCriterion = SemanticCriterion | ExtractionCriterion;
 216: 
 217: /**
 218:  * One oracle, decomposed into named criteria.
 219:  *
 220:  * `materialSlots` names every piece of text the rubric may see, and `judge()` refuses material
 221:  * that does not match the declaration exactly â€” a missing slot would silently make a criterion
 222:  * unanswerable, and an undeclared one would let a caller feed the judge text no rubric mentions.
 223:  *
 224:  * `version` is a human-facing marker only. Recordings are invalidated by the request hash
 225:  * (`oracles.ts`), never by this string, because a hand-maintained version constant is exactly the
 226:  * mechanism a rubric edit forgets to bump (Gate 1 finding F6).
 227:  */
 228: export type Rubric = {
 229:   id: string;
 230:   version: string;
 231:   materialSlots: string[];
 232:   criteria: RubricCriterion[];
 233: };
 234: 
 235: /** How the k repeated votes split on one criterion. `pass + fail` always equals the vote count. */
 236: export type VoteTally = {
 237:   pass: number;
 238:   fail: number;
 239: };
 240: 
 241: export type CriterionVerdict = {
 242:   criterionId: string;
 243:   pass: boolean;
 244:   /** a short verbatim quote, taken from the first vote that agreed with the majority */
 245:   evidence: string;
 246:   votes: VoteTally;
 247: };
 248: 
 249: /**
 250:  * What a verdict is worth, attached to the verdict itself.
 251:  *
 252:  * `requestedModel` and `servedModels` are deliberately two fields: a fixed model id is a pinned
 253:  * snapshot, not a pinned behaviour, so the id we asked for and the id the provider says it served
 254:  * are two facts and drift between them has to be visible rather than assumed away.
 255:  */
 256: export type VerdictProvenance = {
 257:   /** 'replay' = committed recordings, no network; 'live' = a real judge call this run */
 258:   source: 'live' | 'replay';
 259:   requestedModel: string;
 260:   /** `response.model` per vote, in vote order */
 261:   servedModels: string[];
 262:   /** the full-request hash per vote, in vote order â€” the replay key */
 263:   requestHashes: string[];
 264:   rubricId: string;
 265:   rubricVersion: string;
 266:   effort: string;
 267:   votes: number;
 268: };
 269: 
 270: export type SemanticVerdict = {
 271:   /** every REQUIRED criterion passed */
 272:   pass: boolean;
 273:   criteria: CriterionVerdict[];
 274:   provenance: VerdictProvenance;
 275: };
 276: 
 277: /**
 278:  * The seam a suite reaches. One method, because everything else about the oracle â€” which model,
 279:  * how many votes, live or replayed â€” is the harness's to decide from the tier and the at-config
 280:  * registry, not a suite's to pass in.
 281:  */
 282: export type SemanticOracle = {
 283:   judge(rubric: Rubric, material: Record<string, string>): Promise<SemanticVerdict>;
 284: };
 285: 
 286: /* ----------------------------------------------------------------------- the harness */
 287: 
 288: /**
 289:  * What `createHarness()` hands a suite. A suite names its own system-under-test map and world
 290:  * type; everything else is the same for all thirty of them.
 291:  *
 292:  * A TYPE ALIAS, NOT AN INTERFACE, and that is load-bearing. An interface is open to declaration
 293:  * merging, so a suite could write `declare module './contracts.ts' { interface AtHarness { auditLog?: string[] } }`
 294:  * and then read `h.auditLog` with a green type-check, against a harness that supplies no such thing.
 295:  * The member being OPTIONAL is what makes it slip through: `createHarness()` still satisfies its
 296:  * annotation, so nothing anywhere goes red. That is the same lie a free harness type parameter used
 297:  * to permit, arriving by a different door and needing no `any` and no suppression. A type alias
 298:  * cannot be merged into, so this door is shut, and `tests/at/typeprobes/` keeps the attack on file
 299:  * as an executable negative test.
 300:  *
 301:  * EVERY CAPABILITY CONTRACT IN THIS FILE IS AN ALIAS FOR THE SAME REASON. Closing only the type
 302:  * below left the identical attack open one level down, and worse there: `sentinels`, `faults`,
 303:  * `static` and `vendors` come from `pendingCapability<T>()`, which casts a Proxy `as T`, so a
 304:  * merged-in member did not break `index.ts` even when it was REQUIRED â€” where the same member added
 305:  * to this type fails with TS2741. `ConfigRegistry` in `config.ts` is an alias for the same reason.
 306:  *
 307:  * So the rule is: contracts are type aliases, never interfaces. It covers everything reachable from
 308:  * the harness object AND the objects `open()` hands a test body â€” `OpenWorld`, `AtContext` and
 309:  * `WorldLike` in `registry.ts` obey it for the same reason these do. `WorldLike` was briefly
 310:  * excluded as belonging to AI4DEV-31's seam; that conflated two different defects. The suite's `W`
 311:  * being an unverified claim was AI4DEV-31's, and that item derived the seam types from the fixture
 312:  * adapter that produces them, leaving a suite no type argument to name either one with â€” though a
 313:  * widened context can still be REBUILT by hand out of the derived types, which is measured and
 314:  * documented on `SeamOpenWorld` in `registry.ts`. The interface being augmentable is this defect,
 315:  * not that one â€” and `w` is handed to the body exactly as `h` is.
 316:  *
 317:  * `tests/at/typeprobes/` carries an attack for every protected type, and
 318:  * `type-invention.selftest.ts` fails by name if any of them becomes an interface again.
 319:  */
 320: export type AtHarness<Sut = Record<string, unknown>, W extends WorldSeam = WorldSeam, Channel extends string = string> = {
 321:   tier: Tier;
 322:   /**
 323:    * Capability names this harness STUBBED for the running tier (e.g. 'vendors.email',
 324:    * 'sut.notifications'). MUST be empty above `loop` â€” otherwise an integration-tier run,
 325:    * which is the /pm-done gate, can silently stub the thing it is gating.
 326:    */
 327:   stubbedCapabilities(): Promise<string[]>;
 328:   clock: Clock;
 329:   fixtures: Fixtures<W>;
 330:   sentinels: Sentinels;
 331:   faults: Faults;
 332:   static: StaticScan;
 333:   config: ConfigRegistry;
 334:   vendors: Vendors<Channel>;
 335:   /**
 336:    * REQUIRED, like every other capability: a suite that reaches for it and finds nothing must fail
 337:    * to compile, not read `undefined`. At `loop` this is a REPLAY of committed recordings and is
 338:    * reported as a stand-in; above `loop` it is the live judge and is reported as real. See
 339:    * `index.ts` for why that split establishes this capability's provenance and nothing more.
 340:    */
 341:   oracles: SemanticOracle;
 342:   sut: Sut;
 343:   /** REQUIRED, not optional: frozen clocks, vendor counters and fault state leak without it */
 344:   teardown(): Promise<void>;
 345: };

 succeeded in 551ms:
 241:   }
 242:   if (!Number.isInteger(votes)) {
 243:     return `a judge vote count of ${votes} is not a whole number of votes â€” there is no honest way to cast ${votes} of them`;
 244:   }
 245:   if (votes < 1) {
 246:     return `a judge vote count of ${votes} casts no votes at all, so every criterion would be decided by nothing`;
 247:   }
 248:   if (votes % 2 === 0) {
 249:     return (
 250:       `a judge vote count of ${votes} is even, so a criterion can split evenly and the majority is undefined â€” ` +
 251:       `whichever way the tie were broken would be a verdict nobody chose`
 252:     );
 253:   }
 254:   return null;
 255: }
 256: 
 257: function comparatorProblem(criterionId: string, compare: CriterionComparator): string | null {
 258:   if (compare.op === 'numeric_within_tolerance') {
 259:     if (!Number.isFinite(compare.expected)) {
 260:       return `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} compares against a non-finite expected value (${String(compare.expected)})`;
 261:     }
 262:     if (!Number.isFinite(compare.tolerance) || compare.tolerance < 0) {
 263:       return (
 264:         `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} has a tolerance of ${String(compare.tolerance)} â€” a tolerance is a ` +
 265:         `non-negative distance, and a negative one would fail every extraction including an exact match`
 266:       );
 267:     }
 268:     return null;
 269:   }
 270:   if (compare.op === 'count_at_least') {
 271:     if (!Number.isInteger(compare.minimum) || compare.minimum < 0) {
 272:       return (
 273:         `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} has a minimum of ${String(compare.minimum)} â€” a count-minimum is a ` +
 274:         `non-negative whole number`
 275:       );
 276:     }
 277:     return null;
 278:   }
 279:   return `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} carries an unknown comparator ${JSON.stringify((compare as { op: string }).op)}`;
 280: }
 281: 
 282: /**
 283:  * A rubric is refused at construction, not discovered mid-verdict. A duplicate criterion id would
 284:  * make one criterion's answer silently overwrite another's, and an empty criteria list would
 285:  * produce `pass: true` from a judge that was never asked anything â€” the most expensive false green
 286:  * available here.
 287:  */
 288: export function rubricProblem(rubric: Rubric): string | null {
 289:   if (typeof rubric?.id !== 'string' || rubric.id.trim() === '') return 'a rubric must carry a non-empty id';
 290:   if (typeof rubric.version !== 'string' || rubric.version.trim() === '') {
 291:     return `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} carries no version`;
 292:   }
 293:   if (!Array.isArray(rubric.materialSlots) || rubric.materialSlots.length === 0) {
 294:     return `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} declares no material slots, so the judge would be shown nothing to judge`;
 295:   }
 296:   const slotsSeen = new Set<string>();
 297:   for (const slot of rubric.materialSlots) {
 298:     if (typeof slot !== 'string' || slot.trim() === '') {
 299:       return `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} declares a blank material slot`;
 300:     }
 301:     if (slotsSeen.has(slot)) {
 302:       return `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} declares the material slot ${JSON.stringify(slot)} twice`;
 303:     }
 304:     slotsSeen.add(slot);
 305:   }
 306:   if (!Array.isArray(rubric.criteria) || rubric.criteria.length === 0) {
 307:     return (
 308:       `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} has no criteria â€” a verdict over zero criteria passes without ` +
 309:       `anything having been judged`
 310:     );
 311:   }
 312:   const idsSeen = new Set<string>();
 313:   for (const criterion of rubric.criteria) {
 314:     // The id and the kind are READ OFF THE VALUE FIRST, before any narrowing. The types say a
 315:     // criterion is one of two shapes; this function exists for the case where the value disagrees
 316:     // with its type â€” a rubric assembled from JSON, or a suite that wrote one by hand â€” and inside
 317:     // the exhausted branch below TypeScript has narrowed the value to `never`, where nothing can
 318:     // be read at all.
 319:     const criterionId: string = criterion?.id;
 320:     const kind: string = criterion?.kind;
 321:     if (typeof criterionId !== 'string' || criterionId.trim() === '') {
 322:       return `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} carries a criterion with no id`;
 323:     }
 324:     if (idsSeen.has(criterionId)) {
 325:       return (
 326:         `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} carries the criterion id ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} twice â€” ` +
 327:         `one of the two answers would silently replace the other`
 328:       );
 329:     }
 330:     idsSeen.add(criterionId);
 331:     if (typeof criterion.statement !== 'string' || criterion.statement.trim() === '') {
 332:       return `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} has no statement, so the judge would be asked nothing`;
 333:     }
 334:     if (typeof criterion.required !== 'boolean') {
 335:       return `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} does not say whether it is required`;
 336:     }
 337:     if (kind === 'extraction') {
 338:       const extraction = criterion as ExtractionCriterion;
 339:       if (typeof extraction.extract?.what !== 'string' || extraction.extract.what.trim() === '') {
 340:         return `extraction criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} does not say what to extract`;
 341:       }
 342:       const problem = comparatorProblem(criterionId, extraction.compare);
 343:       if (problem !== null) return problem;
 344:     } else if (kind !== 'semantic') {
 345:       return `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} has an unknown kind ${JSON.stringify(kind)}`;
 346:     }
 347:   }
 348:   return null;
 349: }
 350: 
 351: /**
 352:  * The material must match the rubric's declaration exactly. A MISSING slot leaves a criterion
 353:  * unanswerable while the judge answers anyway; an UNDECLARED one feeds the judge text the rubric
 354:  * never mentions, which is a different oracle wearing this one's id.
 355:  */
 356: export function materialProblem(rubric: Rubric, material: Record<string, string>): string | null {
 357:   if (material === null || typeof material !== 'object') return 'material must be a record of slot name to text';
 358:   const supplied = Object.keys(material);
 359:   const missing = rubric.materialSlots.filter((slot) => !supplied.includes(slot));
 360:   if (missing.length) {
 361:     return `rubric ${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} declares material slot${missing.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} ${missing.join(', ')} that the caller did not supply`;
 362:   }
 363:   const undeclared = supplied.filter((slot) => !rubric.materialSlots.includes(slot));
 364:   if (undeclared.length) {
 365:     return (
 366:       `${undeclared.join(', ')} ${undeclared.length === 1 ? 'is' : 'are'} not declared by rubric ` +
 367:       `${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)} â€” declared slots: ${rubric.materialSlots.join(', ')}`
 368:     );
 369:   }
 370:   for (const slot of rubric.materialSlots) {
 371:     if (typeof material[slot] !== 'string' || material[slot].trim() === '') {
 372:       return `material slot ${JSON.stringify(slot)} is empty, so every criterion about it would be answered from nothing`;
 373:     }
 374:   }
 375:   return null;
 376: }
 377: 
 378: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------- rendering and hashing */
 379: 
 380: function renderCriterion(criterion: RubricCriterion): string {
 381:   const head = `CRITERION ${criterion.id} (${criterion.kind})\n  ${criterion.statement.trim()}`;
 382:   if (criterion.kind === 'semantic') return head;
 383:   const parts = [`  extract: ${criterion.extract.what.trim()}`];
 384:   if (criterion.extract.unit) parts.push(`  unit: ${criterion.extract.unit.trim()}`);
 385:   if (criterion.extract.normalization) parts.push(`  normalization: ${criterion.extract.normalization.trim()}`);
 386:   return `${head}\n${parts.join('\n')}`;
 387: }
 388: 
 389: /**
 390:  * THE COMPARATOR IS NOT IN THIS PROMPT and that omission is the design. Telling the judge
 391:  * "expected 4200, tolerance 420" invites it to answer the comparison rather than perform the
 392:  * extraction, and then the arithmetic is back inside the generation where its instability came
 393:  * from. The judge is asked for a number; `compareExtraction()` below owns the threshold.
 394:  */
 395: export function renderSystemPrompt(rubric: Rubric): string {
 396:   return [
 397:     'You are judging whether supplied text satisfies a fixed rubric, as part of an automated acceptance test.',
 398:     '',
 399:     'Answer only from the material below. Do not use outside knowledge about the product it came from.',
 400:     'Answer every criterion exactly once, keyed by its id, and answer no criterion that is not listed.',
 401:     '',
 402:     'For a criterion of kind "semantic", set `holds` to whether its statement is true of the material.',
 403:     'For a criterion of kind "extraction", set `value` to the number the criterion names, under the unit and',
 404:     'normalization it gives. Do not decide whether that number is acceptable: a threshold is applied to it in',
 405:     'code, outside this request.',
 406:     '',
 407:     'For every criterion, set `evidence` to a short verbatim quote from the material. Where your answer rests on',
 408:     'something being absent, quote the passage where it would have appeared.',
 409:     '',
 410:     `RUBRIC ${rubric.id} (version ${rubric.version})`,
 411:     '',
 412:     rubric.criteria.map(renderCriterion).join('\n\n'),
 413:   ].join('\n');
 414: }
 415: 
 416: /** Slots are rendered in the RUBRIC's declared order, never the caller object's key order. */
 417: export function renderMaterial(rubric: Rubric, material: Record<string, string>): string {
 418:   return rubric.materialSlots
 419:     .map((slot) => `<material slot="${slot}">\n${material[slot]}\n</material>`)
 420:     .join('\n\n');
 421: }
 422: 
 423: export function renderJudgeRequest(rubric: Rubric, material: Record<string, string>): JudgeRequest {
 424:   return {
 425:     promptVersion: PROMPT_VERSION,
 426:     rubricId: rubric.id,
 427:     rubricVersion: rubric.version,
 428:     model: JUDGE_MODEL,
 429:     maxTokens: JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS,
 430:     effort: JUDGE_EFFORT,
 431:     outputSchema: VERDICT_SCHEMA,
 432:     system: renderSystemPrompt(rubric),
 433:     messages: [{ role: 'user', content: renderMaterial(rubric, material) }],
 434:   };
 435: }
 436: 
 437: /**
 438:  * Key order is not part of a request's meaning, so it must not be part of its hash â€” otherwise a
 439:  * refactor that reorders a literal invalidates every recording for no reason at all.
 440:  */
 441: export function canonicalJson(value: unknown): string {
 442:   if (value === null || typeof value !== 'object') return JSON.stringify(value) ?? 'null';
 443:   if (Array.isArray(value)) return `[${value.map(canonicalJson).join(',')}]`;
 444:   const entries = Object.entries(value as Record<string, unknown>)
 445:     .filter(([, entryValue]) => entryValue !== undefined)
 446:     .sort(([left], [right]) => (left < right ? -1 : left > right ? 1 : 0));
 447:   return `{${entries.map(([key, entryValue]) => `${JSON.stringify(key)}:${canonicalJson(entryValue)}`).join(',')}}`;
 448: }
 449: 
 450: /**
 451:  * THE REPLAY KEY IS THE WHOLE REQUEST, PLUS THE VOTE INDEX.
 452:  *
 453:  * Model id, max tokens, effort, the output schema, the fully rendered system and user text with
 454:  * every material value in it â€” all of it. Any code change that alters what the judge is asked
 455:  * produces a different key, so recordings go stale by construction rather than by discipline
 456:  * (F6). The vote index is in the key because the k votes send an identical request and are
 457:  * distinguished only by being separate samples; without it, replay would answer all k votes with
 458:  * one recording and the majority would be unanimous by accident.
 459:  */
 460: export function replayKey(request: JudgeRequest, voteIndex: number): string {
 461:   return createHash('sha256').update(canonicalJson({ request, voteIndex }), 'utf8').digest('hex');
 462: }
 463: 
 464: /* ---------------------------------------------------------------------- the replay store */
 465: 
 466: /** Where committed recordings live. Resolved from THIS file: they are harness data, not suite data. */
 467: export const RECORDINGS_DIR: string = resolve(fileURLToPath(new URL('.', import.meta.url)), 'recordings');
 468: 
 469: /**
 470:  * One recorded judge call.
 471:  *
 472:  * `recordedFrom` is the answer to finding F2 â€” "how would anyone know a committed recording came
 473:  * from the judge it claims?". A synthetic entry is a legitimate thing for conformance to write
 474:  * into a temp directory; it is not a legitimate thing to find in the committed store, and both
 475:  * the reader and the writer refuse it there. Nothing in this file can produce a synthetic entry
 476:  * that claims to be live, because `recordedFrom` is set by whoever calls the writer and the only
 477:  * live caller is `record-oracles.ts`, which sets it after a real call returns.
 478:  */
 479: export type RecordingEntry = {
 480:   key: string;

 succeeded in 569ms:
   1: /**
   2:  * H4 â€” the semantic oracle: judging the MEANING of generated text, and being able to say what
   3:  * that judgement is worth.
   4:  *
   5:  * WHAT THE PROBLEM ACTUALLY IS. A gate that asks a language model a question gets a different
   6:  * answer on a different day, and an acceptance test whose answer moves is not an acceptance test.
   7:  * The provider exposes no seed â€” sampling parameters are removed on this model's generation and
   8:  * rejected on the neighbouring one â€” so "run it again and get the same bytes" is not available at
   9:  * any price. That fact closes the ratified spec's first option and forces the guarantee to be
  10:  * stated per tier rather than asserted once:
  11:  *
  12:  *   loop tier          BIT-DETERMINISTIC BY CONSTRUCTION. The oracle is a replay of committed
  13:  *                      recordings keyed on the complete rendered request. No network, no
  14:  *                      credential. A miss is a loud typed error naming the key it wanted â€” never
  15:  *                      a fallthrough to a live call, because a gate that quietly goes live when
  16:  *                      its recording is stale is a gate that reports yesterday's answer as
  17:  *                      today's.
  18:  *   integration/drill  STABILITY-BOUNDED, and the bound is measured rather than claimed. Three
  19:  *                      things buy the stability: the rubric is decomposed into named binary
  20:  *                      criteria instead of one free-form score; every number is EXTRACTED by the
  21:  *                      judge and compared in code below, so arithmetic never depends on
  22:  *                      generation; and each criterion is decided by majority over k repeated
  23:  *                      votes, k from the at-config registry.
  24:  *
  25:  * Majority voting does not make a live call deterministic and this file does not pretend it does
  26:  * (Gate 1 finding F1). It reduces independent variance; correlated drift â€” a model behaving
  27:  * differently next month under the same id â€” is not something k votes can see. What CAN see it is
  28:  * the provenance every verdict carries and the request hash every recording is keyed on, so drift
  29:  * shows up as a changed answer or a stale key rather than as a quiet pass.
  30:  *
  31:  * NO REFUSAL FALLBACK, DELIBERATELY. The API can decline a request, and the documented remedy is
  32:  * to re-run it on another model. Doing that here would swap the oracle's identity mid-suite: the
  33:  * verdict would be a different judge's, with the same green tick. So a refusal, a truncation, or
  34:  * output that does not match the verdict schema is a typed error carrying the cause. An oracle
  35:  * that cannot judge says so; it never passes.
  36:  */
  37:

  59: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------------ the pinned judge */
  60: 
  61: /**
  62:  * ONE MODULE PINS THE JUDGE, so "which model decided this" has a single answer and changing it is
  63:  * a one-line diff plus a re-record.
  64:  *
  65:  * `claude-opus-5`: the judge answers pinned binary questions and does verbatim extraction under a
  66:  * strict output schema, so instruction-following stability is the only quality that matters, and
  67:  * cost is immaterial at this volume (one call carries a whole rubric). Self-preference â€” the
  68:  * product generates with Claude too â€” is neutralized by the rubric decomposition rather than by
  69:  * the model choice: the judge never ranks and never free-scores, it checks named criteria against
  70:  * supplied text.
  71:  */
  72: export const JUDGE_MODEL = 'claude-opus-5';
  73: 
  74: /** One verdict object per call. Thinking is at the model default and is billed inside this cap. */
  75: export const JUDGE_MAX_TOKENS = 4000;
  76: 
  77: /**
  78:  * PROVISIONAL (Gate 1 finding F7). Anthropic's own guidance is to start high and lower effort
  79:  * where task-specific evaluation shows quality holds â€” and there is no consuming evaluation to
  80:  * run that sweep against yet, so any value here is a guess and the honest thing is to label it
  81:  * one. The labelled sweep belongs to the first suite that consumes an oracle. Effort is part of
  82:  * the replay key, so changing it invalidates every recording by construction rather than by
  83:  * anyone remembering to.
  84:  */
  85: export const JUDGE_EFFORT = 'low' satisfies JudgeEffort;
  86: 
  87: /**
  88:  * METADATA ONLY, and the distinction is the whole of finding F6. A hand-bumped prompt version is
  89:  * an invalidation mechanism that fails exactly when it matters: someone edits the prompt and does
  90:  * not bump it, and every recording keeps answering. The real mechanism is `replayKey()`, which
  91:  * hashes the rendered prompt itself. This string survives so a human reading a recording can see
  92:  * which generation of the prompt produced it.
  93:  */
  94: export const PROMPT_VERSION = 'oracle-prompt-1';
  95: 
  96: export type JudgeEffort = 'low' | 'medium' | 'high' | 'xhigh' | 'max';
  97: 
  98: /* --------------------------------------------------------------------------- typed failures */
  99: 
 100: /** Every way this capability can decline to answer. It declines loudly; it never passes. */
 101: export class OracleError extends Error {
 102:   constructor(message: string) {
 103:     super(message);
 104:     this.name = 'OracleError';
 105:   }
 106: }
 107: 
 108: /**
 109:  * The replay store had no entry for a key. Carries the key, because the ONLY useful next step is
 110:  * to record that exact request â€” and because a miss must be visibly different from a live
 111:  * transport with no credential, which is how conformance proves loop tier never falls through.
 112:  */
 113: export class OracleReplayMiss extends OracleError {
 114:   constructor(
 115:     readonly key: string,
 116:     message: string,
 117:   ) {
 118:     super(message);
 119:     this.name = 'OracleReplayMiss';
 120:   }
 121: }
 122: 
 123: /** The live judge cannot run in this process â€” see the message, which names the deferred boundary. */
 124: export class OracleUnavailable extends OracleError {
 125:   constructor(message: string) {
 126:     super(message);
 127:     this.name = 'OracleUnavailable';
 128:   }
 129: }
 130: 
 131: /* ------------------------------------------------------------------------------ the request */
 132: 
 133: /**
 134:  * THE COMPLETE RENDERED REQUEST â€” every input that can change what the judge answers, in one
 135:  * serializable object, because that object is what gets hashed (F6).
 136:  *
 137:  * `rubricId` and `rubricVersion` ride along even though the prompt already contains them in
 138:  * prose: a machine-readable copy lets a recording be identified by a human and, more usefully,
 139:  * makes a rubric renamed-but-otherwise-identical produce a different key.
 140:  */
 141: export type JudgeRequest = {
 142:   promptVersion: string;
 143:   rubricId: string;
 144:   rubricVersion: string;
 145:   model: string;
 146:   maxTokens: number;
 147:   effort: JudgeEffort;
 148:   outputSchema: Record<string, unknown>;
 149:   system: string;
 150:   messages: { role: 'user'; content: string }[];
 151: };
 152: 
 153: /** What a transport hands back. Deliberately bytes-and-metadata: no parsing happens out there. */
 154: export type JudgeResponse = {
 155:   /** where THESE BYTES came from on THIS run â€” never where they were originally produced */
 156:   source: 'live' | 'replay';
 157:   /** `response.model`: what the provider says it served, which is not the same fact as `model` */
 158:   servedModel: string;
 159:   stopReason: string;
 160:   /** the refusal category or truncation detail, when the provider supplied one */
 161:   stopDetail?: string;
 162:   text: string;
 163: };
 164: 
 165: /**
 166:  * The injectable seam. Both real implementations below satisfy it, and so do the fakes in
 167:  * `oracles.selftest.ts` â€” which is what lets the entire conformance wall run offline, with a
 168:  * transport that throws if it is touched standing in for "the network was not reached".
 169:  */
 170: export type JudgeTransport = {
 171:   send(request: JudgeRequest, voteIndex: number): Promise<JudgeResponse>;
 172: };
 173: 
 174: /**
 175:  * The verdict schema, enforced server-side by structured outputs AND re-validated here.
 176:  *
 177:  * Both, not either. Server-side enforcement is what makes the happy path reliable; local
 178:  * re-validation is what catches the case the schema cannot express â€” an answer for a criterion id
 179:  * that is not in this rubric, a missing criterion, an extraction answer to a semantic question.
 180:  * A schema is a shape check, and the thing that would quietly ruin a verdict is a well-shaped
 181:  * answer to the wrong question.
 182:  */
 183: export const VERDICT_SCHEMA: Record<string, unknown> = {
 184:   type: 'object',
 185:   additionalProperties: false,
 186:   required: ['answers'],
 187:   properties: {
 188:     answers: {
 189:       type: 'array',
 190:       items: {
 191:         anyOf: [
 192:           {
 193:             type: 'object',
 194:             additionalProperties: false,
 195:             required: ['criterionId', 'kind', 'holds', 'evidence'],
 196:             properties: {
 197:               criterionId: { type: 'string' },
 198:               kind: { type: 'string', const: 'semantic' },
 199:               holds: { type: 'boolean' },
 200:               evidence: { type: 'string' },
 201:             },
 202:           },
 203:           {
 204:             type: 'object',
 205:             additionalProperties: false,
 206:             required: ['criterionId', 'kind', 'value', 'evidence'],
 207:             properties: {
 208:               criterionId: { type: 'string' },
 209:               kind: { type: 'string', const: 'extraction' },
 210:               value: { type: 'number' },
 211:               evidence: { type: 'string' },
 212:             },
 213:           },
 214:         ],
 215:       },
 216:     },
 217:   },
 218: };
 219: 
 220: /** One vote's answer to one criterion, after the local re-validation above. */
 221: export type JudgeAnswer =
 222:   | { criterionId: string; kind: 'semantic'; holds: boolean; evidence: string }
 223:   | { criterionId: string; kind: 'extraction'; value: number; evidence: string };
 224: 
 225: /* --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- guards */

 227: /**
 228:  * These predicates live here rather than in `guards.ts` on purpose. That file holds the checks
 229:  * every SUITE would otherwise re-assert â€” sentinel quality, fault arming â€” and centralizes them
 230:  * because thirty suites would drift. These are the oracle capability's own construction checks:
 231:  * one implementation reads them, and they are exercised both directly and through that
 232:  * implementation below, which is the same standard `guards.ts` is held to.
 233:  *
 234:  * Each returns plain-words trouble, or null when the thing is acceptable.
 235:  */

 237: /** A vote count that cannot decide anything must be refused, never rounded into one that can. */

 239:   if (typeof votes !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(votes)) {
 240:     return `the judge vote count must be a number, got ${JSON.stringify(votes)}`;

 729: 
 730: /** Local re-validation of one vote's structured output. Anything unexpected is an error, never a pass. */
 731: export function parseJudgeAnswers(rubric: Rubric, text: string): JudgeAnswer[] {
 732:   let parsed: unknown;
 733:   try {
 734:     parsed = JSON.parse(text);
 735:   } catch (err) {
 736:     throw new OracleError(
 737:       `the judge's output is not JSON (${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}). The verdict schema is ` +
 738:         `enforced server-side, so unparseable output means the response was truncated or the request was not the ` +
 739:         `one this code believes it sent.`,
 740:     );
 741:   }
 742:   const answersRaw = (parsed as { answers?: unknown })?.answers;
 743:   if (!Array.isArray(answersRaw)) throw new OracleError("the judge's output carries no `answers` array");
 744:   const answers: JudgeAnswer[] = [];
 745:   for (const raw of answersRaw as Record<string, unknown>[]) {
 746:     if (raw === null || typeof raw !== 'object') throw new OracleError('the judge returned an answer that is not an object');
 747:     const criterionId = raw.criterionId;
 748:     const evidence = raw.evidence;
 749:     if (typeof criterionId !== 'string' || criterionId.trim() === '') {
 750:       throw new OracleError('the judge returned an answer with no criterion id');
 751:     }
 752:     if (typeof evidence !== 'string' || evidence.trim() === '') {
 753:       throw new OracleError(`the judge returned no evidence for criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)}`);
 754:     }
 755:     if (raw.kind === 'semantic') {
 756:       if (typeof raw.holds !== 'boolean') {
 757:         throw new OracleError(`the judge's semantic answer for ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} carries no boolean \`holds\``);
 758:       }
 759:       answers.push({ criterionId, kind: 'semantic', holds: raw.holds, evidence });
 760:       continue;
 761:     }
 762:     if (raw.kind === 'extraction') {
 763:       if (typeof raw.value !== 'number' || !Number.isFinite(raw.value)) {
 764:         throw new OracleError(
 765:           `the judge's extraction answer for ${JSON.stringify(criterionId)} carries no finite \`value\` (${String(raw.value)})`,
 766:         );
 767:       }
 768:       answers.push({ criterionId, kind: 'extraction', value: raw.value, evidence });
 769:       continue;
 770:     }
 771:     throw new OracleError(`the judge returned an answer of unknown kind ${JSON.stringify(raw.kind)}`);
 772:   }
 773:   const problem = answersProblem(rubric, answers);
 774:   if (problem !== null) throw new OracleError(problem);
 775:   return answers;
 776: }
 777: 
 778: /**
 779:  * THE ARITHMETIC, IN CODE. This is the half of the determinism story that does not depend on the
 780:  * judge behaving the same way twice: given the same extracted number, this returns the same answer
 781:  * forever.
 782:  */
 783: export function compareExtraction(criterion: ExtractionCriterion, value: number): boolean {
 784:   const compare = criterion.compare;
 785:   if (compare.op === 'numeric_within_tolerance') {
 786:     // INCLUSIVE at the boundary: a tolerance of 0.5 means half a unit either way is acceptable,
 787:     // and "acceptable" that excludes its own stated limit is a different tolerance.
 788:     return Math.abs(value - compare.expected) <= compare.tolerance;
 789:   }
 790:   if (!Number.isInteger(value)) {
 791:     // A COUNT IS NOT ROUNDED. "2.5 occurrences" is the judge failing to count, and rounding it to
 792:     // whichever side happens to pass is the guess this harness exists to refuse.
 793:     throw new OracleError(
 794:       `criterion ${JSON.stringify(criterion.id)} counts occurrences and the judge extracted ${value}, which is not a ` +
 795:         `whole number of them`,
 796:     );
 797:   }
 798:   return value >= compare.minimum;
 799: }
 800: 
 801: function voteOutcome(criterion: RubricCriterion, answer: JudgeAnswer): boolean {
 802:   if (criterion.kind === 'semantic') {
 803:     if (answer.kind !== 'semantic') throw new OracleError(`criterion ${criterion.id} was answered as ${answer.kind}`);
 804:     return answer.holds;
 805:   }
 806:   if (answer.kind !== 'extraction') throw new OracleError(`criterion ${criterion.id} was answered as ${answer.kind}`);
 807:   return compareExtraction(criterion, answer.value);
 808: }
 809: 
 810: /* -------------------------------------------------------------------------- the oracle */
 811: 
 812: export const JUDGE_VOTES_KEY = 'harness.oracle.judge_votes';
 813: 
 814: /**
 815:  * THE REGISTRY IS THE SOLE WRITER of the vote count (finding F10). The number is never a literal
 816:  * in this file: it is read here, from the at-config registry, and `oracles.selftest.ts` proves an
 817:  * override changes both how many times the transport is called and which way a split criterion
 818:  * lands. A literal that happened to equal the registry's value would pass a test that only checked
 819:  * the registry entry existed; it fails the test that overrides it.
 820:  */
 821: export function readJudgeVotes(config: ConfigRegistry): number {
 822:   const votes = config.get<number>(JUDGE_VOTES_KEY);
 823:   const problem = judgeVoteCountProblem(votes);
 824:   if (problem !== null) {
 825:     throw new OracleError(`refusing to build the semantic oracle from ${JUDGE_VOTES_KEY}: ${problem}`);
 826:   }
 827:   return votes;
 828: }
 829: 
 830: export function createSemanticOracle(opts: { transport: JudgeTransport; votes: number }): SemanticOracle {
 831:   const problem = judgeVoteCountProblem(opts.votes);
 832:   if (problem !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing to build the semantic oracle: ${problem}`);
 833:   const votes = opts.votes;
 834: 
 835:   return {
 836:     async judge(rubric, material) {
 837:       const rubricTrouble = rubricProblem(rubric);
 838:       if (rubricTrouble !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing to judge: ${rubricTrouble}`);
 839:       const materialTrouble = materialProblem(rubric, material);
 840:       if (materialTrouble !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing to judge: ${materialTrouble}`);
 841: 
 842:       const request = renderJudgeRequest(rubric, material);
 843:       const ballots: JudgeAnswer[][] = [];
 844:       const servedModels: string[] = [];
 845:       const requestHashes: string[] = [];
 846:       const sources = new Set<JudgeResponse['source']>();
 847: 
 848:       for (let voteIndex = 0; voteIndex < votes; voteIndex++) {
 849:         const response = await opts.transport.send(request, voteIndex);
 850:         // THE STOP REASON IS CHECKED BEFORE THE TEXT IS TOUCHED, and it is checked here rather
 851:         // than in the transport so that a RECORDED refusal replays as a refusal. A recording of a
 852:         // declined call is a legitimate thing to hold; treating it as a verdict is not.
 853:         if (response.stopReason !== 'end_turn') {
 854:           throw new OracleError(
 855:             `the judge did not finish: stop reason ${JSON.stringify(response.stopReason)}` +
 856:               `${response.stopDetail ? ` (${response.stopDetail})` : ''} on vote ${voteIndex} of rubric ` +
 857:               `${JSON.stringify(rubric.id)}. This is an oracle ERROR, never a pass and never a silent retry on ` +
 858:               `another model â€” a substituted model would be a different judge wearing this one's verdict.`,
 859:           );
 860:         }
 861:         if (response.source !== 'live' && response.source !== 'replay') {
 862:           throw new OracleError(`a judge transport reported an unknown source ${JSON.stringify(response.source)}`);
 863:         }
 864:         sources.add(response.source);
 865:         servedModels.push(response.servedModel);
 866:         requestHashes.push(replayKey(request, voteIndex));
 867:         ballots.push(parseJudgeAnswers(rubric, response.text));
 868:       }
 869: 
 870:       if (sources.size !== 1) {
 871:         throw new OracleError(
 872:           `the ${votes} votes did not come from one place (${[...sources].sort().join(', ')}) â€” a verdict half ` +
 873:             `replayed and half live cannot honestly report either`,
 874:         );
 875:       }
 876: 
 877:       const criteria: CriterionVerdict[] = rubric.criteria.map((criterion) => {
 878:         const outcomes = ballots.map((ballot) => {
 879:           const answer = ballot.find((entry) => entry.criterionId === criterion.id)!;
 880:           return { outcome: voteOutcome(criterion, answer), evidence: answer.evidence };
 881:         });
 882:         const passCount = outcomes.filter((entry) => entry.outcome).length;
 883:         // k is a positive ODD integer, so this is a strict majority with no tie to break.
 884:         const pass = passCount * 2 > votes;
 885:         return {
 886:           criterionId: criterion.id,
 887:           pass,
 888:           // The evidence shown is the first vote that AGREED with the majority: quoting a
 889:           // dissenting vote beside the reported verdict would read as the verdict's own support.
 890:           evidence: outcomes.find((entry) => entry.outcome === pass)!.evidence,
 891:           votes: { pass: passCount, fail: votes - passCount },
 892:         };
 893:       });
 894: 
 895:       const provenance: VerdictProvenance = {
 896:         source: [...sources][0]!,
 897:         requestedModel: request.model,
 898:         servedModels,
 899:         requestHashes,
 900:         rubricId: rubric.id,
 901:         rubricVersion: rubric.version,
 902:         effort: request.effort,
 903:         votes,
 904:       };
 905: 
 906:       const failedRequired = criteria.filter(
 907:         (verdict) => !verdict.pass && rubric.criteria.find((criterion) => criterion.id === verdict.criterionId)!.required,
 908:       );
 909:       return { pass: failedRequired.length === 0, criteria, provenance };
 910:     },
 911:   };
 912: }
 913: 
 914: /**
 915:  * The capability, per tier â€” and exactly what that does and does not establish (finding F3).
 916:  *
 917:  * At `loop` the oracle is a REPLAY and says so: `standInCapability` puts `oracles.judge` on the
 918:  * provenance ledger, which is what stops an integration-tier run grading against committed bytes.
 919:  * Above `loop` it is the live judge and is reported real.
 920:  *
 921:  * WHAT THIS DOES NOT DO is make the integration tier reachable. `createHarness()` still registers
 922:  * the clock, the fixtures, the vendor sim and every system-under-test member as stand-ins, so
 923:  * `registry.ts` refuses a non-loop run wholesale today. This settles ONE capability's provenance;
 924:  * the tier becomes reachable when the other stand-ins are replaced, which is other slices' work.
 925:  *
 926:  * `recordingsDir` and `transport` are conformance seams and the harness passes neither. Note that
 927:  * `recordingsDir` is consulted only at loop tier: the test that hands it a directory containing a
 928:  * MATCHING recording at integration tier, and still gets the live transport's refusal, is what
 929:  * proves live mode never consults the replay store.
 930:  */
 931: export function createOracleCapability(opts: {
 932:   tier: Tier;
 933:   config: ConfigRegistry;
 934:   recordingsDir?: string;
 935:   transport?: JudgeTransport;
 936: }): Capability<SemanticOracle> {
 937:   const votes = readJudgeVotes(opts.config);
 938:   if (opts.tier === 'loop') {
 939:     const transport = opts.transport ?? createReplayTransport(opts.recordingsDir ?? RECORDINGS_DIR);
 940:     return standInCapability('oracles.judge', createSemanticOracle({ transport, votes }));
 941:   }
 942:   const transport = opts.transport ?? createLiveTransport();
 943:   return realCapability('oracles.judge', createSemanticOracle({ transport, votes }));
 944: }

   8: import type { AtHarness, StaticScan } from './contracts.ts';

  16: interface FixtureAdapter {
  17:   fixtures: { world(name: string): Promise<{ teardown(): Promise<void> }> };
  18:   sut: Record<string, unknown>;
  19:   /**
  20:    * OPTIONAL, and refused at use rather than ignored. The runner's own black-box trees write
  21:    * disposable adapters that export `sut`, `fixtures` and `teardown` and nothing else, so a
  22:    * required member here would break them at run time. Absence is not permission to no-op: an
  23:    * adapter that offers no fault seam exposes no fault points, so `faults.at()` refuses through
  24:    * `faultPointProblem` in the guard's own words, and a scan of any scope is refused the same way.
  25:    */
  26:   faults?: AdapterFaultSeam;
  27:   sentinels?: AdapterSentinelSeam;
  28:   teardown(): Promise<void>;
  29: }
  30: 
  31: interface FixtureAdapterModule {
  32:   /** the requirement this adapter declares itself to be, e.g. 'req-016' â€” see loadAdapter() */
  33:   requirement: string;
  34:   createFixtureAdapter(opts: {
  35:     clock: ControlledClock;
  36:     worlds: FixtureWorldStore;
  37:     config: ConfigRegistry;
  38:     /**
  39:      * The SUT-facing half of H5's provider seam. ADDITIVE on the options object rather than a new
  40:      * required export, so the runner's disposable black-box adapters â€” which take an options object
  41:      * they largely ignore â€” keep working untouched.
  42:      */
  43:     vendors: { email: EmailProviderPort };
  44:   }): Promise<FixtureAdapter> | FixtureAdapter;
  45: }
  46: 
  47: /**
  48:  * The adapter is resolved through REPO_ROOT rather than relative to this file, so that the suites
  49:  * directory the harness loads from is the SAME tree the runner and the bijection checker read.
  50:  * They agree by construction, including when `AT_REPO_ROOT` points all three at a disposable
  51:  * fixture tree for the runner's own black-box tests.
  52:  */
  53: function adapterUrl(requirement: string): string {
  54:   return pathToFileURL(join(REPO_ROOT, 'tests', 'at', 'suites', requirement, '_fixture.ts')).href;
  55: }
  56: 
  57: async function loadAdapter(
  58:   requirement: string,
  59:   clock: ControlledClock,
  60:   worlds: FixtureWorldStore,
  61:   config: ConfigRegistry,
  62:   vendors: { email: EmailProviderPort },
  63: ): Promise<FixtureAdapter> {
  64:   const moduleUrl = adapterUrl(requirement);
  65:   let module: Partial<FixtureAdapterModule>;
  66:   try {
  67:     module = (await import(/* @vite-ignore */ moduleUrl)) as Partial<FixtureAdapterModule>;
  68:   } catch (err) {
  69:     const detail = err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err);
  70:     throw new Error(`no fixture adapter for ${requirement} at ${moduleUrl} â€” ${detail}`);
  71:   }
  72:   if (typeof module.createFixtureAdapter !== 'function') {
  73:     throw new Error(`fixture adapter for ${requirement} exports no createFixtureAdapter()`);
  74:   }
  75: 
  76:   /*
  77:    * THE ADAPTER SAYS WHO IT IS, AND IS HELD TO IT.
  78:    *
  79:    * `harness/suite-adapters.ts` reads a suite's system-under-test and world types from the module
  80:    * its map names, while THIS function loads a module from a path built out of a string. Those were
  81:    * two independent facts: a mistyped map entry, a renamed directory or an `AT_REPO_ROOT` pointing
  82:    * somewhere else would have made the type-check describe one suite while the run drove another,
  83:    * and every layer would have looked correct on its own.
  84:    *
  85:    * The map entry is constrained to match this literal at compile time; the same literal is checked
  86:    * here against the requirement actually requested. So the key, the module the types came from and
  87:    * the module really imported are one self-declared value, checked at both ends.
  88:    *
  89:    * A MISSING literal is an error too, not a skipped check â€” a guard that switches itself off when a
  90:    * field is absent is the hole again, arriving through the door marked convenience.
  91:    */
  92:   if (module.requirement !== requirement) {
  93:     throw new Error(
  94:       `fixture adapter at ${moduleUrl} declares requirement ` +
  95:         `${module.requirement === undefined ? '<nothing: it exports no `requirement`>' : JSON.stringify(module.requirement)} ` +
  96:         `but was loaded as ${JSON.stringify(requirement)} â€” the suite's types would be read off one ` +
  97:         `module while the run drove another. Add \`export const requirement = ${JSON.stringify(requirement)} as const;\` ` +
  98:         `to that file, or correct whichever of the two names is wrong.`,
  99:     );
 100:   }
 101: 
 102:   return module.createFixtureAdapter({ clock, worlds, config, vendors });
 103: }
 104: 
 105: /**
 106:  * The return type is annotated, not inferred, and that annotation is load-bearing: it is what makes
 107:  * the shared contract a checked promise rather than a hopeful one. Inferred, this factory produced
 108:  * `object` for every pending seam â€” `pendingCapability<T extends object>()` has no inference site,
 109:  * so `T` fell back to its constraint â€” and the result was not assignable to `AtHarness` at all.
 110:  * With the annotation, dropping or misnaming a contract member is a compile error here, where it is
 111:  * written, instead of a surprise in whichever suite reaches for it.
 112:  */
 113: export async function createHarness(opts: {
 114:   requirement: string;
 115:   tier: Tier;
 116:   configOverrides?: ConfigOverrides;
 117: }): Promise<AtHarness> {
 118:   const clock = standInCapability('clock.controlled', new ControlledClock());
 119:   const worlds = new FixtureWorldStore(createFixtureSeed());
 120:   // REAL, not a stand-in: `atconfig.ts` IS the registry of pinned values, so what a test reads
 121:   // here is the article itself. Marking it stand-in would tell an integration-tier run that the
 122:   // gate is grading a substitute, which would be a lie in the other direction.
 123:   const config = realCapability('config.registry', createConfigRegistry(opts.configOverrides));
 124:   // BUILT BEFORE THE ADAPTER, because the adapter is handed the sending half of it. One sim per
 125:   // harness, so the forced-outcome queue and the accepted-identity set cannot survive into the next
 126:   // test â€” `registry.ts` opens a fresh harness per `open()` and tears it down per id.
 127:   const provider = createEmailProviderSim();
 128:   const adapter = await loadAdapter(opts.requirement, clock.value, worlds, config.value, { email: provider.port });
 129:   const fixtures = standInCapability('fixtures.worlds', adapter.fixtures);
 130:   // REAL, not stand-ins, for the same reason `config.registry` is. `stubbedCapabilities()` reports
 131:   // what the HARNESS substituted, and nothing about H3 is substituted here â€” the marker store and
 132:   // the fault router below are the article. What is a substitute in a loop-tier run is the product,
 133:   // and `sut.notifications` already declares that; declaring H3 a stand-in as well would count the
 134:   // same substitution twice and blame the wrong layer for it. It would also cost something real:
 135:   // `registry.ts` refuses ANY stubbed capability above the loop tier, so the label would bar this
 136:   // identical machinery from ever running at integration tier â€” the tier that is the closing gate.
 137:   const sentinels = realCapability('sentinels.planted', createSentinels(adapter.sentinels));
 138:   const faults = realCapability('faults.injection', createFaults(adapter.faults));
 139:   // A STAND-IN BY NATURE, and saying so has teeth: `registry.ts` refuses any stubbed capability
 140:   // above the loop tier, so an integration-tier run cannot silently grade against the simulator.
 141:   // What stands here at that tier is a later slice's decision, not this one's.
 142:   const vendors = standInCapability('vendors.email', { email: provider.sim });
 143:   // H4's semantic oracle, and its provenance is decided by the tier rather than by this file: a
 144:   // REPLAY of committed recordings at loop (a stand-in, and it says so), the live judge above.
 145:   // `oracles.ts` explains what that settles â€” this capability's provenance â€” and what it does not:
 146:   // integration stays unreachable while the clock, fixtures, vendors and every SUT member above are
 147:   // still stand-ins, so this is not a claim that the gate has become runnable.
 148:   //
 149:   // The vote count comes from the at-config registry through `config.value`, which is why this is
 150:   // built after it. An override naming an unusable count fails HERE, at `createHarness()`, rather
 151:   // than at whichever test first judged something.
 152:   const oracles = createOracleCapability({ tier: opts.tier, config: config.value });
 153:   const sutCapabilities: Capability<unknown>[] = Object.entries(adapter.sut).map(([name, value]) =>
 154:     standInCapability(`sut.${name}`, value),
 155:   );
 156:   const constructed: Capability<unknown>[] = [clock, fixtures, config, sentinels, faults, vendors, oracles, ...sutCapabilities];
 157: 
 158:   let tornDown = false;
 159:   return {
 160:     tier: opts.tier,
 161:     stubbedCapabilities: async () => stubbedCapabilityNames(constructed),
 162:     clock: clock.value,
 163:     fixtures: fixtures.value,
 164:     sut: adapter.sut,
 165:     sentinels: sentinels.value,

 174:     vendors: vendors.value,
 175:     oracles: oracles.value,
 176:     config: config.value,

 178:       if (tornDown) return;
 179:       tornDown = true;
 180:       await adapter.teardown();
 181:       await worlds.teardown();
 182:     },

 479: export type RecordingEntry = {
 480:   key: string;
 481:   voteIndex: number;
 482:   recordedAt: string;
 483:   recordedFrom: 'live' | 'synthetic';
 484:   request: JudgeRequest;
 485:   servedModel: string;
 486:   stopReason: string;
 487:   stopDetail?: string;
 488:   text: string;
 489: };
 490: 
 491: /** Is this the committed store â€” the one directory whose entries must all be live? */
 492: export function isCommittedRecordingsDir(dir: string): boolean {
 493:   return resolve(dir) === RECORDINGS_DIR;
 494: }
 495: 
 496: /**
 497:  * Everything wrong with one recording, from the store's point of view.
 498:  *
 499:  * The key check is the load-bearing one and it is not decoration: the store indexes by the key the
 500:  * entry DECLARES, so an entry could otherwise name any key it liked and answer a request it was
 501:  * never a recording of. Re-deriving the hash from the stored request makes a hand-edited key a
 502:  * refusal instead of a silent hit.
 503:  */
 504: export function recordingProblem(entry: RecordingEntry, opts: { committed: boolean }): string | null {
 505:   if (entry === null || typeof entry !== 'object') return 'a recording must be an object';
 506:   if (typeof entry.key !== 'string' || entry.key.trim() === '') return 'a recording carries no key';
 507:   if (!Number.isInteger(entry.voteIndex) || entry.voteIndex < 0) {
 508:     return `recording ${entry.key} carries a nonsensical vote index (${String(entry.voteIndex)})`;
 509:   }
 510:   if (entry.recordedFrom !== 'live' && entry.recordedFrom !== 'synthetic') {
 511:     return (
 512:       `recording ${entry.key} does not say how it was produced (recordedFrom=${JSON.stringify(entry.recordedFrom)}) â€” ` +
 513:       `a recording with no provenance cannot be told from an invented one`
 514:     );
 515:   }
 516:   if (opts.committed && entry.recordedFrom !== 'live') {
 517:     return (
 518:       `recording ${entry.key} is marked ${JSON.stringify(entry.recordedFrom)} and sits in the COMMITTED store, whose ` +
 519:       `entries must every one be the output of a real judge call. A synthetic recording here would make the loop ` +
 520:       `tier green against a verdict no judge ever gave.`
 521:     );
 522:   }
 523:   if (entry.request === null || typeof entry.request !== 'object') return `recording ${entry.key} carries no request`;
 524:   if (typeof entry.servedModel !== 'string' || entry.servedModel.trim() === '') {
 525:     return `recording ${entry.key} does not say which model served it`;
 526:   }
 527:   if (typeof entry.stopReason !== 'string' || entry.stopReason.trim() === '') {
 528:     return `recording ${entry.key} does not say why the judge stopped`;
 529:   }
 530:   if (typeof entry.text !== 'string') return `recording ${entry.key} carries no response text`;
 531:   if (typeof entry.recordedAt !== 'string' || entry.recordedAt.trim() === '') {
 532:     return `recording ${entry.key} carries no recording timestamp`;
 533:   }
 534:   const derived = replayKey(entry.request, entry.voteIndex);
 535:   if (derived !== entry.key) {
 536:     return (
 537:       `recording ${entry.key} declares a key its own stored request does not hash to (${derived}) â€” the entry has ` +
 538:       `been edited by hand, or the request was recorded under different code`
 539:     );
 540:   }
 541:   return null;
 542: }
 543: 
 544: /** What the writer is given. The key and the timestamp are the writer's to set, never a caller's. */
 545: export type RecordingDraft = Omit<RecordingEntry, 'key' | 'recordedAt'>;
 546: 
 547: /**
 548:  * Atomic: written beside the target and renamed into place, so a crash mid-write cannot leave a
 549:  * half-parsed recording that the store would then refuse for the wrong reason.
 550:  *
 551:  * ONE WRITER for both the recorder and conformance (F2). Conformance exercising the same code
 552:  * into a temp directory is what keeps the replay path tested while the committed store is empty.
 553:  */
 554: export function writeRecording(dir: string, draft: RecordingDraft): RecordingEntry {
 555:   const entry: RecordingEntry = {
 556:     ...draft,
 557:     key: replayKey(draft.request, draft.voteIndex),
 558:     recordedAt: new Date().toISOString(),
 559:   };
 560:   const problem = recordingProblem(entry, { committed: isCommittedRecordingsDir(dir) });
 561:   if (problem !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing to write a recording: ${problem}`);
 562:   mkdirSync(dir, { recursive: true });
 563:   const target = join(dir, `${entry.key}.json`);
 564:   const scratch = `${target}.writing-${process.pid}`;
 565:   writeFileSync(scratch, `${JSON.stringify(entry, null, 2)}\n`, 'utf8');
 566:   renameSync(scratch, target);
 567:   return entry;
 568: }
 569: 
 570: /** Every recording in a directory, indexed by key. Refuses the whole directory on any bad entry. */
 571: export function readRecordings(dir: string): Map<string, RecordingEntry> {
 572:   const committed = isCommittedRecordingsDir(dir);
 573:   let files: string[];
 574:   try {
 575:     files = readdirSync(dir).filter((name) => name.endsWith('.json'));
 576:   } catch {
 577:     // A missing directory is an EMPTY store, not a crash: with no judge credential this item ships
 578:     // the directory empty on purpose, and the useful error is the miss below, which names the key.
 579:     return new Map();
 580:   }
 581:   const entries = new Map<string, RecordingEntry>();
 582:   for (const name of files.sort()) {
 583:     const path = join(dir, name);
 584:     let parsed: RecordingEntry;
 585:     try {
 586:       parsed = JSON.parse(readFileSync(path, 'utf8')) as RecordingEntry;
 587:     } catch (err) {
 588:       throw new OracleError(`recording ${path} is not readable JSON â€” ${err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err)}`);
 589:     }
 590:     const problem = recordingProblem(parsed, { committed });
 591:     if (problem !== null) throw new OracleError(`refusing the replay store at ${dir}: ${problem} (${name})`);
 592:     entries.set(parsed.key, parsed);
 593:   }
 594:   return entries;
 595: }
 596: 
 597: /**
 598:  * The loop-tier transport: committed bytes, no network, no credential.
 599:  *
 600:  * The index is read ONCE per instance and never written, which is what makes the oracle stateless
 601:  * per harness in the sense finding F12 asked for â€” `createHarness()` builds a fresh one, so
 602:  * nothing a test does can reach the next test through here, and there is no resource to dispose.
 603:  */
 604: export function createReplayTransport(dir: string): JudgeTransport {
 605:   let index: Map<string, RecordingEntry> | null = null;
 606:   return {
 607:     async send(request, voteIndex) {
 608:       index ??= readRecordings(dir);
 609:       const key = replayKey(request, voteIndex);
 610:       const entry = index.get(key);
 611:       if (!entry) {
 612:         throw new OracleReplayMiss(
 613:           key,
 614:           `no committed recording for judge request ${key} (rubric ${request.rubricId} v${request.rubricVersion}, ` +
 615:             `vote ${voteIndex}) in ${dir}. This is NOT a reason to call the judge live: a loop-tier oracle that ` +
 616:             `reached the network would report today's answer under yesterday's expectations. Record it parent-side ` +
 617:             `with \`bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts\` (needs AT_JUDGE_API_KEY in the CALLING process) and ` +
 618:             `commit the result.`,
 619:         );
 620:       }
 621:       // The source is REWRITTEN to 'replay', never carried over from the recording. The recording
 622:       // says where the bytes were produced; the verdict must say where they came from this run,
 623:       // and reporting 'live' for a replayed call is the exact lie provenance exists to prevent.
 624:       return {
 625:         source: 'replay',
 626:         servedModel: entry.servedModel,
 627:         stopReason: entry.stopReason,
 628:         ...(entry.stopDetail === undefined ? {} : { stopDetail: entry.stopDetail }),
 629:         text: entry.text,
 630:       };
 631:     },
 632:   };
 633: }

 635: /* ---------------------------------------------------------------------- the live transport */
 636: 
 637: /**
 638:  * PARENT-SIDE ONLY. `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` is read from whatever process calls this and is never
 639:  * spread into a child â€” the runner's environment allowlist is untouched by this item, and this
 640:  * item's live surface is the recorder and the smoke script, both of which run beside the developer
 641:  * rather than inside a test (plan Â§4d, ruling F8).
 642:  *
 643:  * The credential is read at CALL time, not construction, so building the capability above the loop
 644:  * tier costs nothing and the refusal below arrives where a suite could actually see it.
 645:  *
 646:  * UNEXERCISED AT THE TIME OF WRITING. No judge credential existed when this landed, so no live
 647:  * call has ever been made through this function. What IS verified is the request shape: it is
 648:  * assembled through the official SDK's own parameter types, so `bun run typecheck` fails if the
 649:  * pinned model, max-token, effort or structured-output fields are not what that SDK version
 650:  * accepts. That is a compile-time check of the shape, not a runtime check of the behaviour, and
 651:  * the difference is stated here rather than glossed.
 652:  */
 653: export function createLiveTransport(): JudgeTransport {
 654:   let client: Anthropic | null = null;
 655:   return {
 656:     async send(request, voteIndex) {
 657:       // Every vote sends the IDENTICAL request; they differ only by being separate samples. The
 658:       // index reaches the key, not the wire.
 659:       void voteIndex;
 660:       const apiKey = process.env.AT_JUDGE_API_KEY?.trim();
 661:       if (!apiKey) {
 662:         throw new OracleUnavailable(
 663:           'the semantic oracle has no judge credential in this process. AT_JUDGE_API_KEY is read only by the ' +
 664:             'PARENT-side recorder and smoke scripts and is deliberately not passed into any test child: how a ' +
 665:             'credential should reach a child at integration tier â€” a broker, a scoped key, something else â€” is a ' +
 666:             'decision deferred to the slice that makes the integration tier real, taken with its first consuming ' +
 667:             'run rather than guessed at now (AI4DEV-20 plan Â§2, ruling F8). At loop tier the oracle replays ' +
 668:             'committed recordings and needs no credential at all.',
 669:         );
 670:       }
 671:       if (!client) {
 672:         const { default: AnthropicClient } = await import('@anthropic-ai/sdk');
 673:         client = new AnthropicClient({ apiKey });
 674:       }
 675:       const message = await client.messages.create({
 676:         model: request.model,
 677:         max_tokens: request.maxTokens,
 678:         system: request.system,
 679:         messages: request.messages,
 680:         output_config: {
 681:           effort: request.effort,
 682:           format: { type: 'json_schema', schema: request.outputSchema },
 683:         },
 684:       });
 685:       const text = message.content
 686:         .filter((block): block is Extract<typeof block, { type: 'text' }> => block.type === 'text')
 687:         .map((block) => block.text)
 688:         .join('');
 689:       return {
 690:         source: 'live',
 691:         servedModel: message.model,
 692:         stopReason: message.stop_reason ?? '<none reported>',
 693:         ...(message.stop_details?.category ? { stopDetail: message.stop_details.category } : {}),
 694:         text,
 695:       };
 696:     },
 697:   };
 698: }

 105: /** One vote's answers to a rubric: criterion id â†’ `holds` for semantic, extracted number otherwise. */
 106: type Ballot = Record<string, boolean | number>;
 107: 
 108: function ballotText(rubric: Rubric, ballot: Ballot, evidenceTag: string): string {
 109:   return JSON.stringify({
 110:     answers: rubric.criteria.map((criterion) =>
 111:       criterion.kind === 'semantic'
 112:         ? {
 113:             criterionId: criterion.id,
 114:             kind: 'semantic',
 115:             holds: ballot[criterion.id] as boolean,
 116:             evidence: `${evidenceTag}:${criterion.id}`,
 117:           }
 118:         : {
 119:             criterionId: criterion.id,
 120:             kind: 'extraction',
 121:             value: ballot[criterion.id] as number,
 122:             evidence: `${evidenceTag}:${criterion.id}`,
 123:           },
 124:     ),
 125:   });
 126: }
 127: 
 128: type ScriptedTransport = { transport: JudgeTransport; calls: number[] };
 129: 
 130: /**
 131:  * A transport driven by a script of ballots, one per vote. Everything the real transports supply â€”
 132:  * the source, the served model, the stop reason â€” is settable, because each of those is a fact a
 133:  * verdict reports and every one of them has a test below that turns it into a refusal.
 134:  */
 135: function scripted(
 136:   rubric: Rubric,
 137:   ballots: Ballot[],
 138:   opts: { source?: 'live' | 'replay'; stopReason?: string; stopDetail?: string; servedModel?: string; text?: string[] } = {},
 139: ): ScriptedTransport {
 140:   const calls: number[] = [];
 141:   return {
 142:     calls,
 143:     transport: {
 144:       async send(_request, voteIndex) {
 145:         calls.push(voteIndex);
 146:         const ballot = ballots[Math.min(voteIndex, ballots.length - 1)]!;
 147:         return {
 148:           source: opts.source ?? 'replay',
 149:           servedModel: opts.servedModel ?? JUDGE_MODEL,
 150:           stopReason: opts.stopReason ?? 'end_turn',
 151:           ...(opts.stopDetail === undefined ? {} : { stopDetail: opts.stopDetail }),
 152:           text: opts.text?.[voteIndex] ?? ballotText(rubric, ballot, `vote-${voteIndex}`),
 153:         };
 154:       },
 155:     },
 156:   };
 157: }
 158: 
 159: /** Stands where "the network was not reached" needs proving: any use at all fails the test. */
 160: const NEVER_TOUCHED: JudgeTransport = {
 161:   async send() {
 162:     throw new Error('this transport must never be reached');
 163:   },
 164: };
 165: 
 166: function oracleWith(transport: JudgeTransport, votes = 3) {
 167:   return createSemanticOracle({ transport, votes });
 168: }

 174:   return dir;
 175: }
 176: afterAll(() => {
 177:   for (const dir of tempDirs) rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
 178: });
 179: 
 180: /**
 181:  * Run a body with no judge credential in this process, whatever the developer has exported.
 182:  *
 183:  * The conformance wall must be incapable of making a real API call, and the live transport reads
 184:  * its credential at call time â€” so the only way to guarantee that is to take it away here and put
 185:  * it back afterwards. What the tests below then assert is exactly the state a test child is
 186:  * guaranteed by the runner's environment allowlist: no key, and a refusal that names why.
 187:  */
 188: async function withoutJudgeCredential<T>(body: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
 189:   const saved = process.env.AT_JUDGE_API_KEY;
 190:   delete process.env.AT_JUDGE_API_KEY;
 191:   try {
 192:     return await body();
 193:   } finally {
 194:     if (saved !== undefined) process.env.AT_JUDGE_API_KEY = saved;
 195:   }
 196: }

 200: describe('a rubric that cannot be judged honestly is refused at construction', () => {

 203:     expect(problem, 'an empty rubric was accepted â€” every material would pass it').toContain('no criteria');
 204:   });
 205: 
 206:   it('refuses a duplicated criterion id, because one answer would silently replace the other', () => {
 207:     const duplicated = rubricProblem({ ...NEUTRAL, criteria: [NEUTRAL.criteria[0]!, NEUTRAL.criteria[0]!] }) ?? '';
 208:     expect(duplicated).toContain('twice');

 213:     expect(rubricProblem({ ...NEUTRAL, criteria: [{ ...NEUTRAL.criteria[0]!, statement: '' }] }) ?? '').toContain(

 223:   it('refuses every unusable comparator payload', () => {
 224:     // The casts are the point: this guard exists for values that disagree with their own type â€” a
 225:     // rubric read from JSON, or one a suite assembled by hand â€” so the payloads below are exactly
 226:     // the ones the compiler would otherwise have caught.
 227:     const withCompare = (compare: unknown): Rubric => ({

 229:       criteria: [{ ...(MEASURED.criteria[0] as ExtractionCriterion), compare: compare as CriterionComparator }],
 230:     });

 232:       'tolerance',
 233:     );
 234:     expect(
 235:       rubricProblem(withCompare({ op: 'numeric_within_tolerance', expected: Number.NaN, tolerance: 1 })) ?? '',
 236:     ).toContain('non-finite');
 237:     expect(rubricProblem(withCompare({ op: 'count_at_least', minimum: 2.5 })) ?? '').toContain('minimum');
 238:     expect(rubricProblem(withCompare({ op: 'count_at_least', minimum: -1 })) ?? '').toContain('minimum');
 239:     expect(rubricProblem(withCompare({ op: 'invented_operator' })) ?? '').toContain('unknown comparator');
 240:   });
