 244:     expect(rubricProblem({ ...NEUTRAL, criteria: [invented] }) ?? '').toContain('unknown kind');
 245:   });
 246: 
 247:   it('refuses material that does not match the declaration, in both directions', () => {

 255:   });

 257:   it('routes judge() through those guards rather than computing them and carrying on', async () => {

 262:     await expect(oracle.judge(NEUTRAL, {})).rejects.toThrow(/did not supply/);

 762:   it('builds a FRESH oracle per harness, so nothing one test replayed reaches the next', async () => {
 763:     const first = await createHarness({ requirement: 'req-016', tier: 'loop' });
 764:     const second = await createHarness({ requirement: 'req-016', tier: 'loop' });
 765:     try {
 766:       expect(first.oracles, 'two harnesses share one oracle instance').not.toBe(second.oracles);
 767:     } finally {
 768:       await first.teardown();
 769:       await second.teardown();
 770:     }
 771: 
 772:     // And the replay index is per instance rather than module-level state: an oracle built over an
 773:     // empty directory keeps seeing it empty, while one built afterwards sees what was written. If
 774:     // the index were shared, the second would inherit the first's emptiness â€” or worse, the first
 775:     // would start answering from bytes it never loaded.
 776:     const dir = tempRecordingsDir();
 777:     const request = renderJudgeRequest(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL);
 778:     const stale = oracleWith(createReplayTransport(dir), 1);
 779:     await expect(stale.judge(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(OracleReplayMiss);
 780:     writeRecording(dir, {
 781:       voteIndex: 0,
 782:       recordedFrom: 'live',
 783:       request,
 784:       servedModel: JUDGE_MODEL,
 785:       stopReason: 'end_turn',
 786:       text: ballotText(NEUTRAL, { alpha: true, beta: true }, 'late'),
 787:     });
 788:     const fresh = oracleWith(createReplayTransport(dir), 1);
 789:     expect((await fresh.judge(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL)).pass).toBe(true);
 790:     await expect(stale.judge(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL)).rejects.toBeInstanceOf(OracleReplayMiss);

 825:       // complete material â€” that the contract can express them at all, which is the claim the
 826:       // ratified acceptance text actually supports today. It does NOT check that the compliant
 827:       // specimen passes and the violating one fails, because that is a claim about the judge and

 829:       // verdict, and a canned verdict is this file grading its own fixture.

 343:       text,
 344:     });
 345: 
 346:   it('replays a recorded verdict bit-for-bit, through the same writer conformance uses', async () => {
 347:     const dir = tempRecordingsDir();

 349:     for (let vote = 0; vote < 3; vote++) {
 350:       writeRecording(dir, liveish(request, vote, ballotText(NEUTRAL, { alpha: true, beta: true }, `rec-${vote}`)));
 351:     }
 352:     const first = await oracleWith(createReplayTransport(dir)).judge(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL);
 353:     const second = await oracleWith(createReplayTransport(dir)).judge(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL);
 354:     expect(first.pass).toBe(true);
 355:     expect(JSON.stringify(second), 'two replays of one recording disagreed').toBe(JSON.stringify(first));
 356:     expect(first.provenance.source, 'a replayed verdict claimed to be live').toBe('replay');
 357:   });

 359:   it('answers a miss with a typed error naming the key, and NEVER falls through to a live call', async () => {
 360:     const dir = tempRecordingsDir();
 361:     // The two failure modes are distinguishable ON PURPOSE. A fallthrough to the live transport
 362:     // would raise OracleUnavailable (no credential in a test child); a refusal to fall through
 363:     // raises OracleReplayMiss. Asserting the class is how "no network was reached" is checked
 364:     // without pretending to observe the socket.
 365:     const thrown = await oracleWith(createReplayTransport(dir))
 366:       .judge(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL)
 367:       .then(() => null, (err: unknown) => err);
 368:     expect(thrown, 'a replay miss did not raise the typed miss').toBeInstanceOf(OracleReplayMiss);
 369:     expect(thrown, 'a loop-tier miss reached the live transport instead of refusing').not.toBeInstanceOf(
 370:       OracleUnavailable,
 371:     );
 372:     expect((thrown as OracleReplayMiss).key).toBe(replayKey(renderJudgeRequest(NEUTRAL, NEUTRAL_MATERIAL), 0));
 373:     expect((thrown as OracleReplayMiss).message, 'the miss does not name the key it wanted').toContain(
 374:       (thrown as OracleReplayMiss).key,

   1: /**
   2:  * The judge recorder â€” `bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts`.
   3:  *
   4:  * PARENT-SIDE, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT. This script is the only thing in the tree that calls
   5:  * the judge for real. It reads `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` from its own environment and runs beside the
   6:  * developer, never inside a test child: the runner's environment allowlist is untouched by this
   7:  * item, so no credential can reach a spawned test, and how one eventually should reach a child at
   8:  * integration tier is a decision deferred to the slice that makes that tier real (plan Â§4d,
   9:  * ruling F8).
  10:  *
  11:  * IT IS ALSO THE SOLE WRITER of committed recordings, which is the answer to Gate 1 finding F2:
  12:  * "how would anyone know a committed recording came from the judge it claims?". Everything it
  13:  * writes is marked `recordedFrom: 'live'` because it has just watched a live response arrive, and
  14:  * `writeRecording()` refuses anything else in the committed directory. Conformance writes through
  15:  * the same function into temp directories with entries marked synthetic, so the replay path stays
  16:  * tested without a single invented byte reaching the store.
  17:  *
  18:  * WHAT IT PRODUCES, in one run:
  19:  *   1. the committed recordings for each example specimen â€” k votes each, keyed on the complete
  20:  *      rendered request, which is what the loop tier replays;
  21:  *   2. a MEASURED stability figure: the full k-vote verdict repeated N times live over the same
  22:  *      fixed specimens, with per-criterion flips counted. Finding F1 accepted the stability
  23:  *      argument only on condition it be measured rather than asserted, and this is where the
  24:  *      measurement comes from;
  25:  *   3. `loop/items/AI4DEV-20/live-smoke.md`, which is that measurement written down.
  26:  *
  27:  * NEVER IN CI. It costs money, it needs a credential CI does not have, and its output is committed
  28:  * data rather than a check.
  29:  *
  30:  * NOT YET RUN. No judge credential existed on the machine where this landed, so every line below
  31:  * is unexercised: what has been verified is that it compiles against the pinned SDK's own request
  32:  * and response types, and nothing more. Treat the first real run as a bring-up, not a formality.
  33:  */
  34: 
  35: import { mkdirSync, writeFileSync } from 'node:fs';
  36: import { join } from 'node:path';
  37: 
  38: import { INSTALL_ROOT } from './check.ts';
  39: import { createConfigRegistry } from './config.ts';
  40: import type { Rubric, SemanticVerdict } from './contracts.ts';
  41: import {
  42:   createLiveTransport,
  43:   createSemanticOracle,
  44:   JUDGE_EFFORT,
  45:   JUDGE_MODEL,
  46:   parseJudgeAnswers,
  47:   readJudgeVotes,
  48:   RECORDINGS_DIR,
  49:   renderJudgeRequest,
  50:   writeRecording,
  51:   type JudgeResponse,
  52:   type JudgeTransport,
  53: } from './oracles.ts';
  54: import { AT_009_07_COMPLIANT, AT_009_07_REJECTION_COPY, AT_009_07_VIOLATING } from './rubrics/at-009-07.ts';
  55: import { AT_004_10_COMPLIANT, AT_004_10_EXAMPLE_RUBRIC, AT_004_10_VIOLATING } from './rubrics/at-004-10.ts';
  56: import { AT_033_07_COMPLIANT, AT_033_07_EXAMPLE_RUBRIC, AT_033_07_VIOLATING } from './rubrics/at-033-07.ts';
  57: 
  58: /** How many times the whole k-vote verdict is repeated to measure how stable it is. */
  59: const STABILITY_REPEATS = 5;
  60: 
  61: type Specimen = {
  62:   label: string;
  63:   rubric: Rubric;
  64:   material: Record<string, string>;
  65:   /** what the specimen was written to be â€” reported beside the verdict, never used to force it */
  66:   expected: 'pass' | 'fail';
  67: };
  68: 
  69: const SPECIMENS: Specimen[] = [
  70:   { label: 'AT-009.07 compliant', rubric: AT_009_07_REJECTION_COPY, material: AT_009_07_COMPLIANT, expected: 'pass' },
  71:   { label: 'AT-009.07 violating', rubric: AT_009_07_REJECTION_COPY, material: AT_009_07_VIOLATING, expected: 'fail' },
  72:   { label: 'AT-004.10 compliant', rubric: AT_004_10_EXAMPLE_RUBRIC, material: AT_004_10_COMPLIANT, expected: 'pass' },
  73:   { label: 'AT-004.10 violating', rubric: AT_004_10_EXAMPLE_RUBRIC, material: AT_004_10_VIOLATING, expected: 'fail' },
  74:   { label: 'AT-033.07 compliant', rubric: AT_033_07_EXAMPLE_RUBRIC, material: AT_033_07_COMPLIANT, expected: 'pass' },
  75:   { label: 'AT-033.07 violating', rubric: AT_033_07_EXAMPLE_RUBRIC, material: AT_033_07_VIOLATING, expected: 'fail' },
  76: ];
  77: 
  78: /**
  79:  * The live transport, wrapped so that the FIRST repeat's responses become the committed
  80:  * recordings and every later repeat is measurement only.
  81:  *
  82:  * Later repeats cannot be recorded even if we wanted them: they send an identical request, so they
  83:  * hash to the same key, and the store holds one entry per key. That is the correct arrangement â€”
  84:  * replay must answer with one fixed set of votes, and the spread across repeats is a fact about
  85:  * the live judge that belongs in the smoke report rather than in the store.
  86:  *
  87:  * The response is VALIDATED before it is written. A refusal, a truncation or output that is not a
  88:  * verdict is a recording nobody should keep: it would replay as an error forever, and the loop tier
  89:  * would inherit a failure that only ever happened once.
  90:  */
  91: function recordingTransport(rubric: Rubric, record: boolean): JudgeTransport {
  92:   const live = createLiveTransport();
  93:   return {
  94:     async send(request, voteIndex) {
  95:       const response: JudgeResponse = await live.send(request, voteIndex);
  96:       if (response.stopReason !== 'end_turn') {
  97:         throw new Error(
  98:           `vote ${voteIndex} did not finish: ${response.stopReason}${response.stopDetail ? ` (${response.stopDetail})` : ''}`,
  99:         );
 100:       }
 101:       // Throws if the output is not a verdict for THIS rubric. Nothing unvalidated is stored.
 102:       parseJudgeAnswers(rubric, response.text);
 103:       if (record) {
 104:         const entry = writeRecording(RECORDINGS_DIR, {
 105:           voteIndex,
 106:           recordedFrom: 'live',
 107:           request,
 108:           servedModel: response.servedModel,
 109:           stopReason: response.stopReason,
 110:           ...(response.stopDetail === undefined ? {} : { stopDetail: response.stopDetail }),
 111:           text: response.text,
 112:         });
 113:         console.log(`    recorded vote ${voteIndex} -> ${entry.key.slice(0, 16)}â€¦ (served by ${response.servedModel})`);
 114:       }
 115:       return response;
 116:     },
 117:   };
 118: }
 119: 
 120: type SpecimenResult = {
 121:   specimen: Specimen;
 122:   verdicts: SemanticVerdict[];
 123:   /** criterion id -> how many of the N repeats disagreed with the first repeat */
 124:   flips: Record<string, number>;
 125:   overallFlips: number;
 126: };
 127: 
 128: async function measure(specimen: Specimen, votes: number): Promise<SpecimenResult> {
 129:   console.log(`  ${specimen.label} (expected ${specimen.expected})`);
 130:   const verdicts: SemanticVerdict[] = [];
 131:   for (let repeat = 0; repeat < STABILITY_REPEATS; repeat++) {
 132:     const oracle = createSemanticOracle({ transport: recordingTransport(specimen.rubric, repeat === 0), votes });
 133:     const verdict = await oracle.judge(specimen.rubric, specimen.material);
 134:     verdicts.push(verdict);
 135:     console.log(`    repeat ${repeat + 1}/${STABILITY_REPEATS}: ${verdict.pass ? 'pass' : 'fail'}`);
 136:   }
 137:   const first = verdicts[0]!;
 138:   const flips: Record<string, number> = {};
 139:   for (const criterion of first.criteria) {
 140:     flips[criterion.criterionId] = verdicts.filter(
 141:       (verdict) => verdict.criteria.find((entry) => entry.criterionId === criterion.criterionId)!.pass !== criterion.pass,
 142:     ).length;
 143:   }
 144:   return {
 145:     specimen,
 146:     verdicts,
 147:     flips,
 148:     overallFlips: verdicts.filter((verdict) => verdict.pass !== first.pass).length,
 149:   };
 150: }
 151: 
 152: function report(results: SpecimenResult[], votes: number): string {
 153:   const lines: string[] = [
 154:     '# AI4DEV-20 â€” live judge smoke and measured stability',
 155:     '',
 156:     `Recorded ${new Date().toISOString()} by \`bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts\`, parent-side.`,
 157:     '',
 158:     `- judge model requested: \`${JUDGE_MODEL}\``,
 159:     `- effort: \`${JUDGE_EFFORT}\` (PROVISIONAL â€” no consuming evaluation exists to settle it)`,
 160:     `- votes per criterion: ${votes} (\`harness.oracle.judge_votes\`)`,
 161:     `- full k-vote verdict repeated ${STABILITY_REPEATS} times per specimen`,
 162:     '',
 163:     'A FLIP is a repeat whose answer differed from the first repeat, on the same fixed specimen and',
 164:     'the same rubric. It is a measurement of this judge on these six specimens on this day â€” not a',
 165:     'bound that holds for any other rubric, and not a claim that a live gate is deterministic. It',
 166:     'cannot see correlated drift: a model that answered differently next month under the same id',
 167:     'would show zero flips here and a changed answer there.',
 168:     '',
 169:     '| specimen | written to | verdicts | overall flips | per-criterion flips |',
 170:     '|---|---|---|---|---|',
 171:   ];
 172:   for (const result of results) {
 173:     const verdicts = result.verdicts.map((verdict) => (verdict.pass ? 'pass' : 'fail')).join(' ');
 174:     const perCriterion = Object.entries(result.flips)
 175:       .map(([id, count]) => `${id}: ${count}`)
 176:       .join('; ');
 177:     lines.push(
 178:       `| ${result.specimen.label} | ${result.specimen.expected} | ${verdicts} | ` +
 179:         `${result.overallFlips}/${STABILITY_REPEATS - 1} | ${perCriterion} |`,
 180:     );
 181:   }
 182:   lines.push(
 183:     '',
 184:     '## Served models',
 185:     '',
 186:     'The id requested and the id served are separate facts, and a divergence between them is the',
 187:     'first sign that a pinned snapshot has stopped being one.',
 188:     '',
 189:     ...[...new Set(results.flatMap((result) => result.verdicts.flatMap((verdict) => verdict.provenance.servedModels)))].map(
 190:       (model) => `- \`${model}\``,
 191:     ),
 192:     '',
 193:     '## Committed recordings',
 194:     '',
 195:     `The first repeat of each specimen was written to \`tests/at/harness/recordings/\` â€” ${votes} votes per`,
 196:     'specimen, keyed on the complete rendered request. Later repeats hash to the same keys by',
 197:     'construction, so only the first set is stored; the spread across repeats is the table above.',
 198:   );
 199:   return `${lines.join('\n')}\n`;
 200: }
 201: 
 202: async function main(): Promise<number> {
 203:   if (!process.env.AT_JUDGE_API_KEY?.trim()) {
 204:     console.error(
 205:       'record-oracles: no AT_JUDGE_API_KEY in this process.\n' +
 206:         '\n' +
 207:         'This script is the only live surface in the tree and it runs beside you, never inside a\n' +
 208:         'test child. Export the key in THIS shell and run it again:\n' +
 209:         '\n' +
 210:         "    $env:AT_JUDGE_API_KEY = '<key>'\n" +
 211:         '    bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts\n' +
 212:         '\n' +
 213:         'Nothing is written without one: an empty recordings directory is the honest state, and\n' +
 214:         'synthetic entries dressed as live ones are refused by the writer anyway.',
 215:     );
 216:     return 2;
 217:   }
 218: 
 219:   const votes = readJudgeVotes(createConfigRegistry());
 220:   console.log(`record-oracles: ${JUDGE_MODEL} at effort ${JUDGE_EFFORT}, ${votes} votes, ${STABILITY_REPEATS} repeats`);
 221:   mkdirSync(RECORDINGS_DIR, { recursive: true });
 222: 
 223:   const results: SpecimenResult[] = [];
 224:   for (const specimen of SPECIMENS) results.push(await measure(specimen, votes));
 225: 
 226:   const target = join(INSTALL_ROOT, 'loop', 'items', 'AI4DEV-20', 'live-smoke.md');
 227:   mkdirSync(join(INSTALL_ROOT, 'loop', 'items', 'AI4DEV-20'), { recursive: true });
 228:   writeFileSync(target, report(results, votes), 'utf8');
 229:   console.log(`\nwrote ${target}`);
 230:   console.log('Commit the recordings and the smoke report together â€” they are one piece of evidence.');
 231: 
 232:   const unexpected = results.filter(
 233:     (result) => (result.verdicts[0]!.pass ? 'pass' : 'fail') !== result.specimen.expected,
 234:   );
 235:   if (unexpected.length) {
 236:     // NOT a failure of the recorder: the specimens are examples, and a judge disagreeing with one
 237:     // is a fact worth knowing rather than an error to swallow. It is reported loudly and the
 238:     // recordings are kept, because the disagreement is the interesting part.
 239:     console.warn(
 240:       `\n${unexpected.length} specimen(s) did not land where they were written to: ` +
 241:         unexpected.map((result) => result.specimen.label).join(', '),
 242:     );
 243:   }
 244:   return 0;
 245: }
 246: 
 247: if (import.meta.main) process.exit(await main());

   1: # Environment variables â€” what goes where.
   2: #
   3: # The tracked `.env` holds PUBLIC values only: the Supabase project id, the project URL, and
   4: # the publishable key. All three are designed to be public â€” they ship in the browser bundle
   5: # and are protected by row-level security, not by secrecy. That file stays in git.
   6: #
   7: # Any SECRET â€” the Supabase service-role key, provider API keys (Anthropic, Stripe, GitHub,
   8: # Linear, Lovable), webhook signing secrets â€” goes in `.env.local`, which is git-ignored and
   9: # is never committed. If a value would do damage in a stranger's hands, it belongs there.
  10: #
  11: # WHY `.env` STAYS TRACKED, against Supabase's own advice (founder decision, 2026-07-29).
  12: # Supabase documents that `config.toml` can pull secrets via `env(NAME)` from the root `.env`,
  13: # and therefore says do not commit it. We keep it tracked anyway, for one reason: Lovable owns
  14: # the frontend and its build needs those VITE_ values present in the repo. The tradeoff is
  15: # accepted knowingly, with two mitigations:
  16: #   1. The test harness no longer inherits the parent environment â€” it passes an allowlisted
  17: #      env to its children, so a secret sitting in `.env`/`.env.local` cannot reach a test and
  18: #      from there reach the hosted project. (See tests/at/harness/runner.ts.)
  19: #   2. The rule above is the discipline that keeps it safe: NOTHING secret goes in `.env`.
  20: #      If a Supabase feature ever needs `env()` interpolation for a real secret, that is the
  21: #      moment to revisit this decision â€” not before.
  22: #
  23: # This file lists the variable names only, with empty values, as the shape to copy.
  24: 
  25: # AT_JUDGE_API_KEY is PARENT-SIDE ONLY, and that is a design decision rather than a convention.
  26: # It is the credential the acceptance harness's semantic oracle (H4) uses to call the judge model,
  27: # and it is read by exactly two things, both of which run beside you rather than inside a test:
  28: # `tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts` and the smoke it writes. The test runner's environment
  29: # allowlist does NOT carry it, so it cannot reach a spawned test process â€” which means it belongs
  30: # in your shell or in `.env.local`, never anywhere a child could inherit it. How a judge credential
  31: # should reach a child at integration tier is deliberately undecided; that call belongs to the
  32: # slice that makes the integration tier real, taken with its first consuming run.
  33: AT_JUDGE_API_KEY=
  34: SUPABASE_PROJECT_ID=
  35: SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY=
  36: SUPABASE_URL=
  37: VITE_SUPABASE_PROJECT_ID=
  38: VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY=
  39: VITE_SUPABASE_URL=

  24:  *      `.env.local` can never reach a test (and a test can never reach the hosted project).

  node_modules\@anthropic-ai\sdk\lib\tools\SessionToolRunner.d.ts:34:     * event resets the countdown and it re-arms

  node_modules\@anthropic-ai\sdk\lib\tools\SessionToolRunner.d.ts:35:     * countdown is deferred while a

  node_modules\@anthropic-ai\sdk\lib\tools\SessionToolRunner.d.ts:36:     * dispatching, and starts fresh once the

 110: /* --------------------------------------------------------------- the child environment (leak) */
 111: 
 112: /**
 113:  * The ONLY variables a child process inherits. Everything else is dropped.
 114:  *
 115:  * WHY AN ALLOWLIST: bun auto-loads `.env` and `.env.local` into this process, and `.env.example`
 116:  * tells developers to put their real secrets in `.env.local`. Spreading `process.env` into the
 117:  * test process would hand every one of those to every test â€” and a test reading a conventional
 118:  * name like `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` would then be pointed at the HOSTED project, which the
 119:  * acceptance suites must never touch. The child gets the platform minimum plus exactly the
 120:  * values this runner validated. Children are additionally launched with bun's `--no-env-file`,
 121:  * so a child does not re-read the env files for itself.
 122:  */
 123: const ENV_ALLOWLIST = [
 124:   // process/platform basics
 125:   'PATH',
 126:   'PATHEXT',
 127:   'COMSPEC',
 128:   'SystemRoot',
 129:   'SystemDrive',
 130:   'windir',
 131:   'OS',
 132:   'NUMBER_OF_PROCESSORS',
 133:   'PROCESSOR_ARCHITECTURE',
 134:   'PROCESSOR_IDENTIFIER',
 135:   'USERNAME',
 136:   'LANG',
 137:   'LC_ALL',
 138:   'TZ',
 139:   // temp + home, which bun, vitest and the Supabase CLI all need in order to write caches
 140:   'TEMP',
 141:   'TMP',
 142:   'TMPDIR',
 143:   'HOME',
 144:   'HOMEDRIVE',
 145:   'HOMEPATH',
 146:   'USERPROFILE',
 147:   'APPDATA',
 148:   'LOCALAPPDATA',
 149:   'PROGRAMDATA',
 150:   'PROGRAMFILES',
 151:   'PROGRAMFILES(X86)',
 152:   'PROGRAMW6432',
 153:   'XDG_CACHE_HOME',
 154:   'BUN_INSTALL',
 155:   // how the Supabase CLI finds the container runtime (never a credential)
 156:   'DOCKER_HOST',
 157:   'DOCKER_CONTEXT',
 158:   'DOCKER_CONFIG',
 159:   'DOCKER_CERT_PATH',
 160: ];
 161: 
 162: /** Windows environment names are case-insensitive, so match that way and keep the parent's casing. */
 163: export function childEnv(extra: Record<string, string> = {}): Record<string, string> {
 164:   const wanted = new Set(ENV_ALLOWLIST.map((name) => name.toLowerCase()));
 165:   const env: Record<string, string> = {};
 166:   for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(process.env)) {
 167:     if (value !== undefined && wanted.has(key.toLowerCase())) env[key] = value;
 168:   }
 169:   return { ...env, ...extra };
 170: }

 175:  * process IS bun, so its own path is the answer; the PATH lookup only matters when something

 657: export interface Message {
 658:     /**
 659:      * Unique object identifier.
 660:      *
 661:      * The format and length of IDs may change over time.
 662:      */
 663:     id: string;
 664:     /**
 665:      * Information about the container used in the request (for the code execution
 666:      * tool)
 667:      */
 668:     container: Container | null;
 669:     /**
 670:      * Content generated by the model.
 671:      *
 672:      * This is an array of content blocks, each of which has a `type` that determines
 673:      * its shape.
 674:      *
 675:      * Example:
 676:      *
 677:      * ```json
 678:      * [{ "type": "text", "text": "Hi, I'm Claude." }]
 679:      * ```
 680:      *
 681:      * If the request input `messages` ended with an `assistant` turn, then the
 682:      * response `content` will continue directly from that last turn. You can use this
 683:      * to constrain the model's output.
 684:      *
 685:      * For example, if the input `messages` were:
 686:      *
 687:      * ```json
 688:      * [
 689:      *   {
 690:      *     "role": "user",
 691:      *     "content": "What's the Greek name for Sun? (A) Sol (B) Helios (C) Sun"
 692:      *   },
 693:      *   { "role": "assistant", "content": "The best answer is (" }
 694:      * ]
 695:      * ```
 696:      *
 697:      * Then the response `content` might be:
 698:      *
 699:      * ```json
 700:      * [{ "type": "text", "text": "B)" }]
 701:      * ```
 702:      */
 703:     content: Array<ContentBlock>;
 704:     /**
 705:      * The model that will complete your prompt.
 706:      *
 707:      * See [models](https://docs.anthropic.com/en/docs/models-overview) for additional
 708:      * details and options.
 709:      */
 710:     model: Model;
 711:     /**
 712:      * Conversational role of the generated message.
 713:      *
 714:      * This will always be `"assistant"`.
 715:      */
 716:     role: 'assistant';
 717:     /**
 718:      * Structured information about a refusal.
 719:      */
 720:     stop_details: RefusalStopDetails | null;
 721:     /**
 722:      * The reason that we stopped.
 723:      *
 724:      * This may be one the following values:
 725:      *
 726:      * - `"end_turn"`: the model reached a natural stopping point
 727:      * - `"max_tokens"`: we exceeded the requested `max_tokens` or the model's maximum
 728:      * - `"stop_sequence"`: one of your provided custom `stop_sequences` was generated
 729:      * - `"tool_use"`: the model invoked one or more tools
 730:      * - `"pause_turn"`: we paused a long-running turn. You may provide the response
 731:      *   back as-is in a subsequent request to let the model continue.
 732:      * - `"refusal"`: when streaming classifiers intervene to handle potential policy
 733:      *   violations
 734:      * - `"model_context_window_exceeded"`: we exceeded the model's context window
 735:      *
 736:      * In non-streaming mode this value is always non-null. In streaming mode, it is
 737:      * null in the `message_start` event and non-null otherwise.
 738:      */
 739:     stop_reason: StopReason | null;
 740:     /**
 741:      * Which custom stop sequence was generated, if any.
 742:      *
 743:      * This value will be a non-null string if one of your custom stop sequences was
 744:      * generated.
 745:      */
 746:     stop_sequence: string | null;
 747:     /**
 748:      * Object type.
 749:      *
 750:      * For Messages, this is always `"message"`.
 751:      */
 752:     type: 'message';
 753:     /**
 754:      * Billing and rate-limit usage.
 755:      *
 756:      * Anthropic's API bills and rate-limits by token counts, as tokens represent the
 757:      * underlying cost to our systems.
 758:      *
 759:      * Under the hood, the API transforms requests into a format suitable for the
 760:      * model. The model's output then goes through a parsing stage before becoming an
 761:      * API response. As a result, the token counts in `usage` will not match one-to-one
 762:      * with the exact visible content of an API request or response.
 763:      *
 764:      * For example, `output_tokens` will be non-zero, even for an empty string response
 765:      * from Claude.
 766:      *
 767:      * Total input tokens in a request is the summation of `input_tokens`,
 768:      * `cache_creation_input_tokens`, and `cache_read_input_tokens`.
 769:      */
 770:     usage: Usage;
 771: }

 210: export type AtConfigKey = keyof typeof AT_CONFIG;

   1: /**
   2:  * The config capability the suites hold â€” a reader over the at-config registry.
   3:  *
   4:  * This is NOT a stand-in for a later slice: the registry in `atconfig.ts` IS the single source
   5:  * for every pinned number, so the thing a test reaches through `h.config` is the real article and
   6:  * is registered as a real capability. There is no future version of it to wait for.
   7:  *
   8:  * Suites address values by DOTTED KEY (`req-015.thread_comment_notifications.max_per_window`)
   9:  * rather than by the registry's TypeScript identifier. The dotted key says which requirement owns
  10:  * the knob and what the knob is, which is what a test author is thinking about; the identifier is
  11:  * an implementation detail of the registry module. The map below is the only place the two meet.
  12:  *
  13:  * Two things throw rather than returning something usable:
  14:  *   - an unknown key, because a typo that silently returned undefined would make a cap of
  15:  *     `undefined` compare as "no cap" and the guard assertion would pass on nothing;
  16:  *   - a `null` value, i.e. a knob the requirement names but nobody has pinned. That doctrine is
  17:  *     the registry's own (`atconfig.ts`): an invented threshold is worse than a red.
  18:  */
  19: 
  20: import { AT_CONFIG, type AtConfigKey } from './atconfig.ts';
  21: import type { ConfigOverrides } from './registry.ts';
  22: 
  23: /**
  24:  * Every dotted key the suites may read, and the registry entry that pins it.
  25:  *
  26:  * The `req-0NN.` prefix names the requirement that owns a knob. `harness.` is the other kind: a
  27:  * knob the TEST MACHINERY owns, which no requirement names because it is not a promise the product
  28:  * makes. Both live in one registry for the same reason â€” the alternative is a literal in the
  29:  * implementation, and then two sources of truth drift apart with both looking correct.
  30:  */
  31: export const CONFIG_KEYS: Record<string, AtConfigKey> = {
  32:   'req-015.thread_comment_notifications.max_per_window': 'threadCommentNotificationsMaxPerWindow',
  33:   'req-015.thread_comment_notifications.window_ms': 'threadCommentNotificationsWindowMs',
  34:   'req-015.thread_comment_notifications.coalesce': 'threadCommentNotificationsCoalesce',
  35:   'harness.oracle.judge_votes': 'oracleJudgeVotes',
  36: };
  37: 
  38: export type ConfigRegistry = {
  39:   get<T>(key: string): T;
  40: };
  41: 
  42: /** Where an override names a knob that does not exist. Empty = every override is addressable. */
  43: export function unknownConfigKeys(overrides: ConfigOverrides): string[] {
  44:   return Object.keys(overrides)
  45:     .filter((key) => !(key in CONFIG_KEYS))
  46:     .sort();
  47: }
  48: 
  49: /**
  50:  * Build the reader, optionally with per-world overrides layered on top.
  51:  *
  52:  * Overrides may RE-TUNE a knob the registry already carries and may never introduce one. A test
  53:  * that could invent a key would be describing a configuration the product has no notion of, and
  54:  * "the implementation honours its configuration" would then be a claim about a knob nobody wired.
  55:  */
  56: export function createConfigRegistry(overrides: ConfigOverrides = {}): ConfigRegistry {
  57:   const unknown = unknownConfigKeys(overrides);
  58:   if (unknown.length) {
  59:     throw new Error(
  60:       `config override${unknown.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} ${unknown.join(', ')} name no at-config entry â€” ` +
  61:         `an override re-tunes a pinned value, it cannot invent one. Known keys: ${Object.keys(CONFIG_KEYS).sort().join(', ')}`,
  62:     );
  63:   }
  64: 
  65:   return {
  66:     get<T>(key: string): T {
  67:       const entryKey = CONFIG_KEYS[key];
  68:       if (!entryKey) {
  69:         throw new Error(
  70:           `no at-config entry is registered for ${JSON.stringify(key)}. Known keys: ${Object.keys(CONFIG_KEYS).sort().join(', ')}`,
  71:         );
  72:       }
  73:       if (key in overrides) return overrides[key] as T;
  74: 
  75:       const entry = AT_CONFIG[entryKey];
  76:       if (entry.value === null) {
  77:         throw new Error(
  78:           `${key} (${entry.name}) is not pinned anywhere â€” ${entry.source}. A test must fail on an ` +
  79:             `unpinned value rather than substitute a guess.`,
  80:         );
  81:       }
  82:       return entry.value as T;
  83:     },
  84:   };
  85: }

   4:  * DISPOSABLE SKELETON, exactly like `at-004-10.ts`. The ratified criterion supplies the SHAPE of
   5:  * each oracle and none of the values: *"a named in-progress task, an open blocker with a known
   6:  * cause and a known awaited action, a recent-activity set of N known events, and a known fuel
   7:  * balance + burn rate whose runway is a defined value: balance Ã· burn"*, judged so that *"the

  13:  *

  23: export function at033_07AssistantRubric(fixture: {
  24:   id: string;
  25:   /** the in-progress task the fixture pins, which the STATUS answer must name as current work */
  26:   currentTask: string;
  27:   /** the blocker's known cause and the known action it is waiting on */
  28:   blockerCause: string;
  29:   blockerAwaitedAction: string;
  30:   /** how many of the fixture's N recent events the progress answer must carry */
  31:   minimumEventsRecalled: number;
  32:   /** balance Ã· burn, and the tolerance the answer is allowed around it */
  33:   runwayValue: number;
  34:   runwayUnit: string;
  35:   runwayTolerance: number;
  36: }): Rubric {
  37:   return {
  38:     id: `at-033-07-assistant-${fixture.id}`,
  39:     version: '1',
  40:     materialSlots: ['status_answer', 'blocker_answer', 'progress_answer', 'runway_answer'],
  41:     criteria: [
  42:       {
  43:         kind: 'semantic',
  44:         id: 'status-names-current-task',
  45:         statement: `The STATUS answer states that the current work is: ${fixture.currentTask}`,
  46:         required: true,
  47:       },
  48:       {
  49:         kind: 'semantic',
  50:         id: 'blocker-names-cause',
  51:         statement: `The BLOCKER answer names this cause of the blocker: ${fixture.blockerCause}`,
  52:         required: true,
  53:       },
  54:       {
  55:         kind: 'semantic',
  56:         id: 'blocker-names-awaited-action',
  57:         statement: `The BLOCKER answer names what the blocker is waiting on: ${fixture.blockerAwaitedAction}`,
  58:         required: true,
  59:       },
  60:       {
  61:         kind: 'extraction',
  62:         id: 'progress-events-recalled',
  63:         statement: 'How many distinct project events from the recent-activity record does the PROGRESS answer report?',
  64:         required: true,
  65:         extract: {
  66:           what: 'the number of distinct project events reported in the progress answer',
  67:           unit: 'events',
  68:           normalization: 'count each event once even if it is mentioned twice; do not count summarising sentences',
  69:         },
  70:         compare: { op: 'count_at_least', minimum: fixture.minimumEventsRecalled },
  71:       },
  72:       {
  73:         // NO-FABRICATION, and it is deliberately its own criterion rather than a clause on the one
  74:         // above. A recall minimum and a fabrication ban pull in opposite directions: an answer that
  75:         // invents two extra events scores BETTER on recall. Scored together, the invention pays for
  76:         // itself.
  77:         kind: 'semantic',
  78:         id: 'progress-introduces-no-fabrications',
  79:         statement:
  80:           'Every project event, name, date and number in the PROGRESS answer appears in the recent-activity record ' +
  81:           'quoted to you. The answer introduces no event, participant or figure that is not there.',
  82:         required: true,
  83:       },
  84:       {
  85:         kind: 'extraction',
  86:         id: 'runway-value-stated',
  87:         statement: 'What remaining runway does the RUNWAY answer state?',
  88:         required: true,
  89:         extract: {
  90:           what: 'the remaining runway the answer states',
  91:           unit: fixture.runwayUnit,
  92:           normalization: `report the number only, in ${fixture.runwayUnit}; if a range is given, report its midpoint`,
  93:         },
  94:         compare: {
  95:           op: 'numeric_within_tolerance',
  96:           expected: fixture.runwayValue,
  97:           tolerance: fixture.runwayTolerance,
  98:         },
  99:       },
 100:     ],
 101:   };
 102: }

 # Environment variables — what goes where.
 #
 # The tracked `.env` holds PUBLIC values only: the Supabase project id, the project URL, and
 # the publishable key. All three are designed to be public — they ship in the browser bundle
 # and are protected by row-level security, not by secrecy. That file stays in git.
 #
 # Any SECRET — the Supabase service-role key, provider API keys (Anthropic, Stripe, GitHub,
 # Linear, Lovable), webhook signing secrets — goes in `.env.local`, which is git-ignored and
 # is never committed. If a value would do damage in a stranger's hands, it belongs there.
 #
 # WHY `.env` STAYS TRACKED, against Supabase's own advice (founder decision, 2026-07-29).
 # Supabase documents that `config.toml` can pull secrets via `env(NAME)` from the root `.env`,
 # and therefore says do not commit it. We keep it tracked anyway, for one reason: Lovable owns
 # the frontend and its build needs those VITE_ values present in the repo. The tradeoff is
 # accepted knowingly, with two mitigations:
 #   1. The test harness no longer inherits the parent environment — it passes an allowlisted
 #      env to its children, so a secret sitting in `.env`/`.env.local` cannot reach a test and
 #      from there reach the hosted project. (See tests/at/harness/runner.ts.)
 #   2. The rule above is the discipline that keeps it safe: NOTHING secret goes in `.env`.
 #      If a Supabase feature ever needs `env()` interpolation for a real secret, that is the
 #      moment to revisit this decision — not before.
 #
 # This file lists the variable names only, with empty values, as the shape to copy.
 
+# AT_JUDGE_API_KEY is PARENT-SIDE ONLY, and that is a design decision rather than a convention.
+# It is the credential the acceptance harness's semantic oracle (H4) uses to call the judge model,
+# and it is read by exactly two things, both of which run beside you rather than inside a test:
+# `tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts` and the smoke it writes. The test runner's environment
+# allowlist does NOT carry it, so it cannot reach a spawned test process — which means it belongs
+# in your shell or in `.env.local`, never anywhere a child could inherit it. How a judge credential
+# should reach a child at integration tier is deliberately undecided; that call belongs to the
+# slice that makes the integration tier real, taken with its first consuming run.
+AT_JUDGE_API_KEY=
 SUPABASE_PROJECT_ID=
 SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY=
 SUPABASE_URL=

 VITE_SUPABASE_URL=

 /**
  * The config capability the suites hold — a reader over the at-config registry.
  *
  * This is NOT a stand-in for a later slice: the registry in `atconfig.ts` IS the single source
  * for every pinned number, so the thing a test reaches through `h.config` is the real article and
  * is registered as a real capability. There is no future version of it to wait for.
  *
  * Suites address values by DOTTED KEY (`req-015.thread_comment_notifications.max_per_window`)
  * rather than by the registry's TypeScript identifier. The dotted key says which requirement owns
  * the knob and what the knob is, which is what a test author is thinking about; the identifier is
  * an implementation detail of the registry module. The map below is the only place the two meet.
  *
  * Two things throw rather than returning something usable:
  *   - an unknown key, because a typo that silently returned undefined would make a cap of
  *     `undefined` compare as "no cap" and the guard assertion would pass on nothing;
  *   - a `null` value, i.e. a knob the requirement names but nobody has pinned. That doctrine is
  *     the registry's own (`atconfig.ts`): an invented threshold is worse than a red.
  */
 
 import { AT_CONFIG, type AtConfigKey } from './atconfig.ts';
 import type { ConfigOverrides } from './registry.ts';
 
-/** Every dotted key the suites may read, and the registry entry that pins it. */
+/**
+ * Every dotted key the suites may read, and the registry entry that pins it.
+ *
+ * The `req-0NN.` prefix names the requirement that owns a knob. `harness.` is the other kind: a
+ * knob the TEST MACHINERY owns, which no requirement names because it is not a promise the product
+ * makes. Both live in one registry for the same reason — the alternative is a literal in the
+ * implementation, and then two sources of truth drift apart with both looking correct.
+ */
 export const CONFIG_KEYS: Record<string, AtConfigKey> = {
   'req-015.thread_comment_notifications.max_per_window': 'threadCommentNotificationsMaxPerWindow',
   'req-015.thread_comment_notifications.window_ms': 'threadCommentNotificationsWindowMs',
   'req-015.thread_comment_notifications.coalesce': 'threadCommentNotificationsCoalesce',
+  'harness.oracle.judge_votes': 'oracleJudgeVotes',
 };
 
 export type ConfigRegistry = {
   get<T>(key: string): T;
 };
 
 /** Where an override names a knob that does not exist. Empty = every override is addressable. */
 export function unknownConfigKeys(overrides: ConfigOverrides): string[] {
   return Object.keys(overrides)
     .filter((key) => !(key in CONFIG_KEYS))
     .sort();
 }
 
 /**
  * Build the reader, optionally with per-world overrides layered on top.
  *
  * Overrides may RE-TUNE a knob the registry already carries and may never introduce one. A test
  * that could invent a key would be describing a configuration the product has no notion of, and
  * "the implementation honours its configuration" would then be a claim about a knob nobody wired.
  */
 export function createConfigRegistry(overrides: ConfigOverrides = {}): ConfigRegistry {
   const unknown = unknownConfigKeys(overrides);
   if (unknown.length) {
     throw new Error(
       `config override${unknown.length === 1 ? '' : 's'} ${unknown.join(', ')} name no at-config entry — ` +
         `an override re-tunes a pinned value, it cannot invent one. Known keys: ${Object.keys(CONFIG_KEYS).sort().join(', ')}`,
     );
   }
 
   return {
     get<T>(key: string): T {
       const entryKey = CONFIG_KEYS[key];
       if (!entryKey) {
         throw new Error(
           `no at-config entry is registered for ${JSON.stringify(key)}. Known keys: ${Object.keys(CONFIG_KEYS).sort().join(', ')}`,
         );
       }
       if (key in overrides) return overrides[key] as T;
 
       const entry = AT_CONFIG[entryKey];
       if (entry.value === null) {
         throw new Error(
           `${key} (${entry.name}) is not pinned anywhere — ${entry.source}. A test must fail on an ` +
             `unpinned value rather than substitute a guess.`,
         );
       }
       return entry.value as T;
     },
   };
 }

Name      Length Attributes
----      ------ ----------
README.md   2135    Archive
# Committed judge recordings â€” deliberately EMPTY

This directory is the loop tier's semantic oracle. Every `*.json` in it is one recorded call to
the pinned judge model, keyed on a SHA-256 of the complete rendered request plus the vote index.
`harness/oracles.ts` replays them; nothing here ever reaches the network.

**It contains no recordings, and that is the honest state rather than an oversight.** No judge
credential (`AT_JUDGE_API_KEY`) existed on the machine where AI4DEV-20 was built, so no live call
was ever made, so there is nothing truthful to commit. Committing synthetic entries dressed as
live ones would be the exact false green the harness exists to remove â€” and the store refuses
them anyway: a recording carries `recordedFrom`, and both the reader and the writer reject
anything but `"live"` in **this** directory. Conformance exercises the identical writer and reader
into temp directories, with entries marked `"synthetic"`, which is why the replay path is tested
while this store is empty.

Until it is filled, any loop-tier `judge()` call fails with a typed miss naming the key it wanted.
That is intended: a loop-tier oracle that quietly went live on a miss would report today's answer
under yesterday's expectations.

## Filling it

Parent-side only â€” the credential is never passed into a test child (see `oracles.ts`, and ruling
F8 in `loop/items/AI4DEV-20/gate1-rulings.md`):

```powershell
$env:AT_JUDGE_API_KEY = '<key>'   # in the calling shell only; never committed, never logged
bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts
```

The recorder canonicalizes the request, calls the judge, validates the response against the
verdict schema, and writes each entry atomically with its provenance. Commit what it writes.

## When they go stale

By construction, not by discipline. The key covers the model id, max tokens, effort, the output
schema and the fully rendered prompt including every material value â€” so any change to what the
judge is asked produces a different key and the old recordings simply stop matching. A stale
recording cannot answer a changed question; it can only fail to be found.

# AI4DEV-20 â€” live judge smoke: NOT RUN

**No live smoke was performed, because no judge credential existed.** `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` was unset
in the executor's environment and no `.env.local` carried one, so the semantic oracle has never
made a real call. Nothing here is a measurement; this file exists so the absence is written down
rather than inferred from a missing file.

Checked, without printing or logging any value: `$env:AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` unset, `$env:ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`
unset, no `.env.local` in the worktree or in the main checkout.

## What that means, concretely

| | state |
|---|---|
| `tests/at/harness/recordings/` | **empty** â€” only its `README.md`. Nothing synthetic was committed. |
| Any loop-tier `judge()` call today | a typed `OracleReplayMiss` naming the key it wanted |
| The live transport | written, compiles against the pinned SDK's own request and response types, **never executed** |
| The recorder | written; its no-credential path was run and exits 2 writing nothing (see below) |
| Measured stability (Gate 1 finding F1) | **absent.** The N=5 repeat measurement needs live calls. |

The recorder's refusal path is the one part of the live surface that was exercised:

```
$ bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts
record-oracles: no AT_JUDGE_API_KEY in this process.
...
exit 2
```

## What is unproved, stated plainly

1. **That a live call succeeds at all.** The request shape is compile-checked against
   `@anthropic-ai/sdk@0.115.0` â€” the pinned model id, `max_tokens`, `output_config.effort` and
   `output_config.format` are assembled through that SDK's own parameter types, so a shape the SDK
   rejects fails `bun run typecheck`. That is a check of the SHAPE, not of the behaviour. Whether
   the judge returns a verdict matching `VERDICT_SCHEMA`, whether `stop_reason` is `end_turn`,
   whether the prompt elicits usable extractions â€” all unknown.
2. **The stability bound.** Finding F1 accepted the k-vote majority argument only on condition the
   stability be measured. It has not been. `record-oracles.ts` is the instrument; it has not been
   pointed at anything.
3. **The effort pin.** `low` is PROVISIONAL and labelled so in `oracles.ts`. The sweep that would
   settle it needs a consuming evaluation, which does not exist either.
4. **The SDK under bun.** The dependency installs and type-checks; it has not been executed.

## The proof point

The first consuming suite's integration run is where all four become answerable, and that is the
same slice that has to decide how a judge credential reaches a test child. Until then the loop
tier is honest about having nothing to replay: it fails loudly with the key it wanted rather than
going live, and the committed store refuses any entry that cannot show live provenance.

**To fill the gap**, parent-side, on a machine with a key:

```powershell
$env:AT_JUDGE_API_KEY = '<key>'
bun tests/at/harness/record-oracles.ts
```

That records k votes per example specimen, repeats the full verdict five times to count flips,
overwrites this file with the measurement, and leaves the recordings to be committed alongside it.

 136: P1. THE SDK'S TYPE DECLARATIONS NOW JOIN THE STRICT ACCEPTANCE PROGRAM.

 140:     choosing: it is clean today. The trade is real in both directions â€” this is
 141:     the ONLY check that exists on the live request shape while no credential does,
 142:     and an SDK upgrade whose declarations do not compile under ES2022-with-no-DOM
 143:     would redden `bun run typecheck` for a reason unrelated to the harness. The
 144:     alternative is a non-analyzable specifier (a variable, as loadAdapter() uses),
 145:     which buys immunity and costs the shape check. RECOMMEND KEEPING AS WRITTEN;
 146:     the version is pinned in bun.lock, so the failure mode arrives only on a
 147:     deliberate upgrade.
 148: 
 149: P2. THE VOTE COUNT IS READ AT CONSTRUCTION, so an unusable override fails at
 150:     createHarness() rather than at whichever test first judged something. The plan
 151:     said the oracle constructs from h.config and this is the literal reading.
 152:     Consequence worth naming: every harness reads that knob, including the
 153:     twenty-nine suites that will never judge anything, and an unpinned or invalid
 154:     value would break all of them at once rather than only the oracle's consumers.
 155:     RECOMMEND KEEPING â€” an override that names a knob and gets it wrong should be
 156:     loud â€” but it is a coupling the item agent should see rather than discover.
 157: 
 158: P3. VALIDATION PREDICATES LIVE IN oracles.ts, NOT guards.ts. Plan Â§4b puts them
 159:     there and I followed it. The argument for guards.ts is real: that file exists
 160:     because a bug in a centralized guard green-lights every suite at once, which
 161:     is precisely this file's situation. The argument against is that guards.ts is
 162:     scoped to checks EVERY SUITE would otherwise re-assert, and these are one
 163:     capability's construction checks. They are exported and tested both directly
 164:     and through the implementation, which is the standard guards.ts is held to.
 165:     RECOMMEND KEEPING; flagged because it is a judgement, not a mechanical step.
 166:

 169:     replay store" could only be argued from the code rather than observed: the
 170:     test needs a directory holding a MATCHING recording for every vote, handed to
 171:     an integration-tier capability that must still refuse. The seam is consulted

 179:     they exist for â€” two votes served by different infrastructure under one pinned
 180:     id. RECOMMEND ACCEPTING as the faithful reading.

 182: P6. THE 'source' FIELD WAS SPLIT IN TWO. Â§4b says recordings carry provenance
 183:     including `source`. As built, JudgeResponse.source is where the bytes came
 184:     from THIS RUN ('live' | 'replay') and RecordingEntry.recordedFrom is how they
 185:     were produced ('live' | 'synthetic'). One field could not do both jobs: the
 186:     replay transport must rewrite the source to 'replay', because a verdict
 187:     replayed from a live recording that reported source 'live' would be claiming a

   3:  *
   4:  * WHY THIS IS NOT `tsc -p a && tsc -p b`: `&&` stops at the first failure, so an error in the app
   5:  * config would prevent the acceptance-test check from ever starting. A command that says nothing at
   6:  * all about `tests/at` reads exactly like one that found it clean â€” which is the same false-green

  25:  *
  26:  * Both of those can silently run a compiler that is not this project's. `bun x` falls back to
  27:  * fetching the package from the registry when it is not installed locally, and `require.resolve`
  28:  * was measured resolving to a globally cached TypeScript 7.0.2 rather than the pinned 5.9.3. A
  29:  * type-check performed by an unknown compiler version is not the check anyone thinks they ran, and
  30:  * silent toolchain drift is exactly what this file exists to stop â€” so a missing compiler is a loud
  31:  * failure, never a quiet substitution.
  32:  */
  33: export function pinnedTsc(root: string): string {
  34:   const tsc = join(root, 'node_modules', 'typescript', 'bin', 'tsc');
  35:   if (!existsSync(tsc)) {
  36:     throw new Error(`the pinned TypeScript compiler is not installed at ${tsc} â€” run \`bun install\` first`);
  37:   }
  38:   return tsc;
  39: }
  40: 
  41: const PROJECTS = [
  42:   { label: 'app', project: 'tsconfig.json' },
  43:   { label: 'acceptance tests', project: 'tests/at/tsconfig.json' },
  44: ] as const;
  45: 
  46: function main(): number {
  47:   let tsc: string;
  48:   try {
  49:     tsc = pinnedTsc(ROOT);
  50:   } catch (err) {
  51:     console.error(err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err));
  52:     return 1;
  53:   }
  54: 
  55:   const failures: string[] = [];
  56: 
  57:   for (const { label, project } of PROJECTS) {
  58:     console.log(`\n=== typecheck: ${label} (${project}) ===`);
  59:     const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [tsc, '--noEmit', '--pretty', 'false', '-p', project], {
  60:       cwd: ROOT,
  61:       stdio: 'inherit',
  62:     });
  63: 
  64:     // A compiler that could not be started, and a run killed by a signal (status null), are both
  65:     // failures. Neither may be reported as a pass â€” and each says WHY, because "typecheck failed"
  66:     // with no reason sends the reader hunting for a type error that does not exist.
  67:     if (result.error) {
  68:       console.error(`${project}: the compiler could not be started â€” ${result.error.message}`);
  69:       failures.push(project);
  70:     } else if (result.signal) {
  71:       console.error(`${project}: the compiler was killed by signal ${result.signal} â€” it did not finish, so this is not a pass`);
  72:       failures.push(project);
  73:     } else if (result.status !== 0) {
  74:       console.error(`${project}: tsc exited ${result.status}`);
  75:       failures.push(project);
  76:     }
  77:   }
  78: 
  79:   if (failures.length) {
  80:     console.error(`\ntypecheck FAILED: ${failures.join(', ')}`);
  81:     return 1;
  82:   }
  83: 
  84:   console.log('\ntypecheck OK: both configs clean');
  85:   return 0;
  86: }
  87: 
  88: if (import.meta.main) process.exit(main());

   1: /**
   2:  * The type-invention attacks, kept dead by an executable test rather than by memory.
   3:  *
   4:  * AI4DEV-24 exists to make `bun run typecheck` mean something. The thing that would quietly undo it
   5:  * is a suite declaring members no runtime value supplies and reading them green â€” a type-check that
   6:  * lies, which is worse than no type-check at all because it is believed.
   7:  *
   8:  * Two families live here now. AI4DEV-24's is HARNESS invention: a suite declaring seams
   9:  * `createHarness()` never produces. AI4DEV-31's is SUT-AND-WORLD invention: a suite declaring what
  10:  * its system under test and its fixture world are, when the adapter that really supplies them says
  11:  * otherwise. None of the attacks needs `any` and none needs a suppression, so grepping for those
  12:  * would not have caught any of them.
  13:  *
  14:  * THE PROBES ARE COMPILED PER FILE, not only as one program, and that is load-bearing. A combined
  15:  * exit code is satisfied by ANY error anywhere: a protection could be removed and the suite would
  16:  * stay green on the strength of its neighbours' diagnostics. Each probe file therefore has its own
  17:  * committed child config, and each protection is asserted against the output of the file that
  18:  * attacks it.
  19:  */
  20: 
  21: import { spawnSync } from 'node:child_process';
  22: import { isAbsolute } from 'node:path';
  23: import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
  24: 
  25: import { INSTALL_ROOT } from './check.ts';
  26: import { pinnedTsc } from '../typecheck.ts';
  27: 
  28: const PROBE_PROJECT = 'tests/at/typeprobes/tsconfig.json';
  29: /** AI4DEV-31's attacks in the API that ALLOWED them â€” the red half of that item's proof. */
  30: const LEGACY_SEAM_PROJECT = 'tests/at/typeprobes/tsconfig.sut-seam-legacy.json';
  31: /** AI4DEV-31's attacks in the API that REPLACED it â€” only meaningful after the change. */
  32: const SEAM_PROJECT = 'tests/at/typeprobes/tsconfig.sut-seam.json';
  33: 
  34: interface ProbeRun {
  35:   status: number | null;
  36:   output: string;
  37:   /** every file tsc actually put in the program â€” the control against an empty-program exit 0 */
  38:   files: string[];
  39: }
  40: 
  41: function typecheckProbes(project: string): ProbeRun {
  42:   // The same pinned compiler `bun run typecheck` uses. A negative test is only meaningful if the
  43:   // compiler rejecting the attack is the compiler that will check everything else.
  44:   //
  45:   // `--listFiles` is not decoration: a tsconfig that matches NO files also exits 0 and reports
  46:   // nothing, so "the attack was rejected" and "the attack was never compiled" are indistinguishable
  47:   // without it. Every per-file assertion below leans on `compiled()` to rule that out.
  48:   const result = spawnSync(
  49:     process.execPath,
  50:     [pinnedTsc(INSTALL_ROOT), '--noEmit', '--pretty', 'false', '--listFiles', '-p', project],
  51:     { cwd: INSTALL_ROOT, encoding: 'utf8' },
  52:   );
  53:   if (result.error) throw new Error(`could not run the compiler over ${project}: ${result.error.message}`);
  54:   const raw = `${result.stdout ?? ''}${result.stderr ?? ''}`;
  55:   const lines = raw.split('\n');
  56:   // `--listFiles` interleaves the program's files with the diagnostics. The listed files are
  57:   // ABSOLUTE, while diagnostics name their file relative to the cwd and their continuation lines are
  58:   // indented â€” so absoluteness separates the two without guessing at message text.
  59:   //
  60:   // The absoluteness test has to be THE RUNNING PLATFORM'S. This read `/^[A-Za-z]:[\\/]/`, which is
  61:   // a Windows path and only a Windows path: on Linux every listed file begins with `/`, none matched,
  62:   // `files` came back empty and the two controls below failed on CI while passing locally â€” the
  63:   // controls doing their job, on the wrong question. `isAbsolute` is win32's on Windows and posix's
  64:   // on Linux, which is the same platform whose compiler printed the line.
  65:   const isListedFile = (line: string) => isAbsolute(line);
  66:   return {
  67:     status: result.status,
  68:     output: lines.filter((line) => !isListedFile(line)).join('\n'),
  69:     files: lines.filter(isListedFile).map((line) => line.trim()),
  70:   };
  71: }
  72: 
  73: /** Did tsc really read this probe? Guards every assertion below against a program matching nothing. */
  74: function compiled(run: ProbeRun, probeFile: string): boolean {
  75:   return run.files.some((file) => file.endsWith(probeFile));
  76: }
  77: 
  78: /**
  79:  * TypeScript reports a blocked merge two different ways: `TS2300 Duplicate identifier 'X'` for a
  80:  * single clash, and one `TS6200` listing every identifier when a file has several at once. Both are
   1: /**
   2:  * Tests OF the harness, not tests run BY it.
   3:  *
   4:  * These deliberately live outside `tests/at/suites/` and depend on nothing that AI4DEV-3 has not
   5:  * built yet: no fixtures, no clock, no vendor sims, no `createHarness`. They import the runner's
   6:  * pure pieces and spawn a real child process, so they run today, on a machine with no stack and
   7:  * no Docker. Run them with `bun run at:selftest`.
   8:  *
   9:  * The leak test is the important one: it proves that a secret sitting in this process's
  10:  * environment â€” which is exactly where bun puts everything in `.env` and `.env.local` â€” does not
  11:  * reach a child. That property is not visible in a diff, only in a spawned process's own view of
  12:  * its environment, so it is asserted against a real child rather than against the code.
  13:  */

# Gate 2 review — AI4DEV-20 — GPT-5

## Verdict

BLOCKING FINDINGS PRESENT — provenance and tier seams can produce a green from synthetic/replayed or live behavior in the wrong tier.

## Findings

1. [severity BLOCKER] — tests/at/harness/oracles.ts:554 — `writeRecording()` trusts caller-supplied `recordedFrom: 'live'`; the selftests already manufacture such “live” records, and the committed-directory test is only lexical at line 492. Synthetic bytes can therefore be written as live evidence and replayed green. Make committed writes private and live-transport-only, canonicalize the real directory path, and test forged provenance/path aliases.

2. [severity BLOCKER] — tests/at/harness/oracles.ts:939 — the public `transport` seam bypasses tier semantics: loop can receive `createLiveTransport()`, while integration can receive `createReplayTransport(dir)` despite being marked real. Split or brand the test seam so each tier rejects the opposite transport, with negative tests for both paths.

3. [severity MAJOR] — tests/at/harness/type-invention.selftest.ts:48 — only one oracle test removes `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY`; this spawned compiler and the direct children at `runner.selftest.ts:203` inherit the parent environment. That breaks the no-credential-in-test-children boundary. Use an explicit stripped environment for every selftest spawn and add an `AT_JUDGE_API_KEY` real-child sentinel.

4. [severity MAJOR] — tests/at/harness/oracles.ts:685 — live response content is read before `stop_reason` is checked. A refusal or truncation can therefore touch malformed content before reaching the typed error path. Check the stop reason first and test with content that throws if accessed.

5. [severity MAJOR] — tests/at/harness/oracles.ts:745 — local “schema re-validation” ignores unknown top-level and answer properties, despite `additionalProperties: false` in `VERDICT_SCHEMA`. Malformed live, replayed, and recorded responses can be accepted. Enforce the full schema locally and test extra fields on every path.

6. [severity MAJOR] — tests/at/harness/oracles.selftest.ts:286 — the replay-key test claims to cover material slot names but changes only a material value. Removing slot names from rendering would leave this test green while distinct requests collide. Add a same-id/version renamed-slot case.

7. [severity MAJOR] — tests/at/harness/oracles.selftest.ts:323 — the comparator-leak test does not assert the actual expected value (`10`) or count minimum (`3`); a leak such as `expected: 10` and `threshold: 3` passes its checks. Use unique comparator sentinels and assert none reach the prompt.

8. [severity MAJOR] — tests/at/harness/oracles.selftest.ts:423 — the P9 cleanup deletes every newly named file, cannot restore an overwritten existing recording, and can leave or delete real data if its `finally` fails or a recorder runs concurrently. Use exclusive writes plus target-specific snapshot/restore under a lock.

Default factory wiring, the `runner.ts` diff, the thirteen protected aliases, SDK request field names, rubric labels/boundary, and the REQ-016 manifest yielded no additional finding.

## Residual risks

- No live request, SDK-under-Bun behavior, or stability measurement has run because recordings remain empty.
- Raw judged material and evidence quotes are not independently hardened against prompt injection or verified as verbatim source text.
